Method: TACO employs a Recognize-Imagine-Label loop: (1) a progress-action model recognizes failure-adjacent states, (2) a visuo-tactile generation model imagines local correction segments, (3) the progress-action model labels them with corrective actions. Post-training uses knowledge-insulated tactile adaptation with advantage-conditioned training.

Result: 44% absolute success rate improvement over the base policy and 32% improvement over the policy without knowledge-insulated tactile adaptation on real-world contact-rich manipulation tasks.

Conclusion: Tactile-aware world models enable scalable, cost-effective VLA post-training for contact-rich manipulation by converting real failures into imagined visuo-tactile corrections while preserving visual-language priors.

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have shown promising generalization in robotic manipulation, but they still struggle with contact-rich tasks, where minor contact perturbations can cause unrecoverable failures that are hard to detect from vision alone. Since these failures are localized rather than task-level semantic errors, tactile-aware corrective post-training offers an efficient way to improve recovery. However, scaling such supervision through human intervention is costly. Recent works have explored world models to synthesize imagined rollouts for policy improvement, but vision-only world models may produce visually plausible yet contact-inconsistent trajectories. We therefore introduce TACO, a tactile-aware world-model-driven framework for scalable VLA post-training in contact-rich manipulation. Given real robot rollouts, TACO follows a Recognize-Imagine-Label loop with a tactile-aware world model: a unified progress-action model recognizes failure-adjacent states using progress estimates, a visuo-tactile generation model imagines local correction segments, and the progress-action model labels them with executable corrective actions. To incorporate tactile corrective supervision into VLA post-training, TACO combines knowledge-insulated tactile adaptation with advantage-conditioned training, enabling the policy to learn from imagined corrections without degrading pretrained visual-language priors. These components enable TACO to convert real-world failures into imagined visuo-tactile corrections for iterative VLA post-training. Experiments on real-world contact-rich manipulation tasks show that TACO achieves 44% absolute success rate improvement over the base policy and 32% over the policy without knowledge-insulated tactile adaptation.

</details>


### [101] [CLEAR: Closed-Loop Reinforcement Learning at Scale for End-to-End Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02841)
*Yunxiao Shi,Hong Cai,Mohammad Ghavamzadeh,Fatih Porikli*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CLEAR introduces closed-loop reinforcement learning for end-to-end autonomous driving with VLA models, using a residual waypoint policy and heterogeneous parallel simulation to achieve state-of-the-art results on CARLA and Bench2Drive.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLA-based autonomous driving policies rely on imitation learning with open-loop training, leading to distribution shift between training and closed-loop inference, which results in suboptimal closed-loop planning performance.

Method: CLEAR proposes: (1) a residual waypoint policy learned via RL on top of pretrained VLA waypoint priors; (2) a heterogeneous pipeline with simulator and VLA learner on separate compute groups to enable massively parallel simulation environments for scalable RL training.

Result: CLEAR significantly outperforms prior methods and achieves new state-of-the-art on CARLA Longest6 v2 and Bench2Drive benchmarks.

Conclusion: Closed-loop RL training with a simple reward, combined with residual policy design and heterogeneous parallel simulation, effectively bridges the open-loop training to closed-loop inference gap for VLA-based E2E-AD.

Abstract: End-to-end autonomous driving (E2E-AD) aims to directly map raw sensor information to driving actions. Recently, with the rapid advancement of multi-modal large language models (MLLMs), researchers have proposed the paradigm of Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models for E2E-AD, where it seeks to integrate visual perception, language understanding and action prediction within a single policy. However, existing VLA-based policies largely adopts imitation learning, where it only learns to drive by optimizing distance-based metrics w.r.t. logged expert trajectories. Such distribution shift between open-loop training and closed-loop inference leads to suboptimal performance in closed-loop planning. To close this gap, we present CLEAR, a system that enables closed-loop training using Reinforcement Learning (RL) at scale for E2E-AD. We propose to learn a novel residual waypoint policy around the waypoint prior from pretrained VLA policies, effectively harnessing the knowledge within. On another front, one of the key challenges to scale up RL for vision-based policies is the number of parallel simulation environments since RL is data hungry. To that end, we design a heterogeneous pipeline that places the simulator and the VLA learner on distinct compute groups, which allows us to dramatically increase the number of simulation environments running in parallel while avoiding resource contention and maintaining training stability. We show that with a simple reward, CLEAR significantly outperforms previous methods and sets new state-of-the-art performance on the challenging benchmarks of CARLA longest6 v2 and Bench2Drive.

</details>


### [102] [Differential Amplifier-Inspired AmpAttention for Multi-View Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02845)
*Jin Yang,Ping Wei,Nanning Zheng*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: AmpAttention (a noise-suppressing attention mechanism inspired by differential amplifiers) and RVAF/RVAF++ models that achieve state-of-the-art success rates on 18 RLBench tasks with 33.3% training time reduction, and excel in high-precision real-world tasks like dart insertion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-view robotic manipulation with attention mechanisms suffers from attention drift caused by redundancy, occlusion, and viewpoint dependency in robotic view images.

Method: Proposes AmpAttention (inspired by differential amplifiers in analog circuits) to suppress attention noise and capture high signal-to-noise ratio signals. Integrates task-guided intra-view and inter-view AmpAttention into the RVAF model, and extends to RVAF++ with SAM2 image encoder.

Result: RVAF achieves optimal average success rate across 18 RLBench tasks (249 variations) with 33.3% training time reduction. RVAF++ achieves 91% success rate on 'insert peg' task.

Conclusion: The proposed methods significantly improve multi-view robotic manipulation performance, especially in high-precision tasks, while reducing training time.

Abstract: Multi-view robotic manipulation methods with the attention mechanism have recently achieved significant progress in both training efficiency and task performance. However, the inherent redundancy, occlusion, and viewpoint dependency in robotic view images often lead to severe attention drift. To address this challenge, we propose AmpAttention, a novel attention mechanism inspired by differential amplifiers in analog circuits. It aims to suppress attention noise and capture high signal-to-noise ratio signals for more reliable perception. Based on this, we introduce the RVAF model, which integrates task-guided intra-view and inter-view AmpAttention. Compared to previous state-of-the-art methods, RVAF achieves the optimal average success rate across 18 RLBench tasks (249 variations) while reducing training time by 33.3\%. RVAF also demonstrates strong potential in real-world high-precision tasks, exemplified by its ability to pick up a dart and accurately insert it into the red bullseye. Furthermore, we extend RVAF to RVAF++ by incorporating the SAM2 image encoder. RVAF++ achieves substantial gains on high-precision tasks, achieving a 91\% success rate on the `insert peg' task. More qualitative results are provided at the anonymous project website https://anonymous.4open.science/w/RVAF-Anonymization.

</details>


### [103] [DREAMSTEER: Latent World Models Can Steer VLA Policies During Deployment Without Any Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02865)
*Hanchen Cui,Sergio Arnaud,Arjun Majumdar,Daniel Dugas,Elie Aljalbout,Karthik Desingh,Krishna Murthy Jatavallabhula,Franziska Meier*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: DREAMSTEER is a deployment-time steering framework for pretrained vision-language-action (VLA) policies that improves robustness and instruction following under distribution shift without any finetuning, using a latent world model and value model to sample, imagine, and rank action candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pretrained VLA policies exhibit promising zero-shot generalization but suffer from deployment-time distribution shift, leading to reduced robustness and inconsistent instruction following. Prior finetuning approaches require in-distribution demonstration data, which is often unavailable.

Method: DREAMSTEER samples candidate action chunks from both the VLA policy and predefined motion primitives, imagines their outcomes using an action-conditioned latent world model, and ranks the imagined trajectories with a language-conditioned value model—all during deployment without finetuning or parameter modifications.

Result: Across four real-world manipulation benchmarks with unseen objects, DREAMSTEER improves task success rate from 23.75% to 66.25% and instruction-following accuracy from 38.75% to 56.25% over the base VLA policy.

Conclusion: DREAMSTEER effectively enhances zero-shot generalization and robustness of pretrained VLA policies under distribution shift by steering at deployment time, eliminating the need for finetuning or environment-specific demonstrations.

Abstract: Pretrained vision-language-action (VLA) policies show promising zero-shot generalization, but often fail under deployment-time distribution shift, leading to decreased robustness and inconsistent instruction following. While prior work commonly tackles this by finetuning on in-distribution data, it assumes demonstrations collected on tasks in the target environment. In this work, we propose DREAMSTEER, a deployment-time steering framework for pretrained VLAs without any finetuning or parameter modifications. The key insight in DREAMSTEER is to leverage a latent world model and a value model to steer pretrained VLA policies. During deployment, DREAMSTEER samples candidate action chunks from a VLA policy and predefined motion primitives, imagines their outcomes using an action-conditioned latent world model, and ranks the imagined trajectories with a language-conditioned value model. Across four real-world manipulation benchmarks with unseen objects, DREAMSTEER improves task success rate from 23.75% to 66.25% and instruction-following accuracy from 38.75% to 56.25% over the base VLA policy.

</details>


### [104] [Continuous-Time Gaussian Belief Trees for Motion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02884)
*Rayan Mazouz,Qi Heng Ho,Zachary N. Sunberg,Morteza Lahijanian*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper presents a sampling-based motion planning framework for continuous-time stochastic systems with process and measurement uncertainty, using an offline hybrid belief propagation model and belief-barrier-function-based safety checking to ensure probabilistic safety across entire trajectory segments, outperforming conventional discrete-time methods in narrow passages.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing sampling-based belief planners typically only check safety at discrete nodes (measurement times), missing inter-sample chance-constraint violations caused by continuous-time uncertainty propagation. The authors aim to provide a principled framework that accounts for continuous-time dynamics and safety requirements.

Method: The robot dynamics are modeled as a continuous-time linear SDE with discrete-time measurements. An offline hybrid belief propagation model is derived, where belief evolves via continuous-time ODEs between measurements and undergoes Kalman filter updates at measurement times. A belief-barrier-function-based safety checker performs segment-level probabilistic verification to certify safety over entire trajectory segments.

Result: The method is integrated with RRT and SST planners and tested across multiple benchmark environments. It achieves high success rates and robust chance-constraint enforcement, particularly in narrow-passage scenarios where discrete-time counterparts fail due to missed inter-sample unsafe behavior.

Conclusion: The proposed framework successfully addresses the gap in continuous-time safety verification for belief-space planning, enabling safer motion planning under uncertainty by detecting inter-sample violations that node-based checks overlook.

Abstract: We address sampling-based motion planning for continuous-time stochastic systems under process and measurement uncertainty, with probabilistic guarantees on safety and performance. The robot dynamics are modeled as a continuous-time linear stochastic differential equation, while sensor measurements arrive at discrete time instants. We derive an offline hybrid belief propagation model in which the belief evolves according to continuous-time ODEs between measurements and undergoes discrete Kalman filter update jumps at measurement times. To ensure safety, we introduce a belief-barrier-function-based safety checker for segment-level probabilistic verification. This enables the planner to certify safety over entire continuous trajectory segments and detect inter-sample chance-constraint violations that are missed by conventional node-based checks. Together, these components provide a principled framework for sampling-based belief planning that accounts for both continuous-time uncertainty propagation and continuous-time safety requirements. We integrate the method with RRT and SST planners and evaluate it across multiple benchmark environments. The results show that the proposed method achieves high success rates and robust enforcement of chance constraints, including in narrow-passage scenarios where discrete-time counterparts fail due to missed inter-sample unsafe behavior.

</details>


### [105] [Longitudinal-Motion-Aware Lateral Control for Autonomous Vehicles: A Robust Nonlinear Control Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02924)
*Sixu Li,Nitesh Kumar,Reyshwanth Ganeshan,Sivakumar Rathinam,Swaroop Darbha,Yang Zhou*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents a longitudinal-motion-aware robust nonlinear lateral control framework for AVs that accounts for varying speed/acceleration and parametric uncertainties, using feedback linearization with two robust designs (Lyapunov redesign and INDI), validated through simulations and real-vehicle tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing lateral controllers rely on constant-speed assumptions and precise vehicle parameters, degrading performance during transient longitudinal maneuvers and under real-world parametric uncertainties.

Method: Derives a tracking error model dependent on varying longitudinal speed/acceleration; uses feedback linearization for linear input-output relation; analyzes internal dynamics for stability; proposes two robust designs: (i) Lyapunov redesign (LR) inspired by sliding mode control, and (ii) incremental nonlinear dynamic inversion (INDI).

Result: Simulations show enhanced tracking accuracy, consistent performance across varying speeds/accelerations, and robustness to model uncertainties. Real-vehicle tests confirm real-time implementation and practical path-tracking performance.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively addresses longitudinal-transient and parametric uncertainty issues in AV lateral control, with rigorous stability proofs and validated practical performance.

Abstract: As autonomous vehicles (AVs) operate in increasingly dynamic traffic conditions, lateral control must be performed while longitudinal speed and acceleration vary. Yet many existing lateral controllers rely on constant-speed or operating-point-based assumptions, which can degrade performance during transient longitudinal maneuvers. Moreover, most methods assume precisely known vehicle parameters, despite real-world parametric uncertainties. To address these limitations, this paper presents a longitudinal-motion-aware robust nonlinear lateral control framework for AVs. It first derives a tracking error model that depends on varying longitudinal speed and acceleration. Using this model, feedback linearization is employed to obtain a linear input-output relation for lateral error tracking while embedding longitudinal motion into the control law. The resulting internal dynamics are then analyzed to ensure overall system stability. To address parameter uncertainty, two robust control designs with distinct implementation trade-offs are proposed: (i) a Lyapunov redesign (LR) approach inspired by sliding mode control, and (ii) an incremental nonlinear dynamic inversion (INDI) method. Both are rigorously analyzed and proven to ensure ultimate boundedness, with key robustness-tuning parameters explicitly identified. Simulations demonstrate enhanced tracking accuracy, consistent performance across varying speeds and accelerations, and robustness to model uncertainties, while also examining the effects of the robustness-related parameters. Real-vehicle tests further confirm real-time implementation and practical path-tracking performance on actual hardware.

</details>


### [106] [Function-Space Diffusion for Motion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02977)
*Zinuo Chang,Yipu Chen,Byoungwoo Park,Hongzhe Yu,Yongxin Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: FSD-MP is a diffusion-based motion planner that models trajectories as continuous functions and performs diffusion in function space using spectral-domain processing with a Matérn covariance and DST-FNO, achieving discretization-invariant trajectory generation that generalizes zero-shot across resolutions up to 16× without retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing diffusion-based motion planners operate on fixed-length waypoint sequences, making them resolution-dependent and preventing zero-shot generalization across different resolutions.

Method: Models trajectories as continuous functions with diffusion in function space; uses a mode-wise forward process in the spectral domain with Matérn-type Gaussian noise; parameterizes the reverse process with a boundary-compatible DST-FNO that preserves start-goal constraints across resolutions.

Result: Competitive planning performance at training resolution; zero-shot generalization across resolutions up to 16× higher with consistent planning behavior and no retraining required.

Conclusion: Function-space diffusion provides an effective framework for discretization-invariant motion planning.

Abstract: Diffusion-based motion planners have demonstrated strong performance in generating diverse and high-quality robot trajectories in cluttered environments with multiple feasible solutions. However, existing approaches typically operate on fixed-length waypoint sequences, making the learned model resolution-dependent, thereby preventing zero-shot generalization across resolutions. In this work, we propose Function-Space Diffusion for Motion Planning (FSD-MP), a diffusion-based motion planner that models trajectories as continuous functions and performs diffusion directly in function space, achieving discretization-invariant trajectory generation. We define a mode-wise forward process in the spectral domain, driven by Gaussian noise with a Matérn-type covariance, and parameterize the reverse process with a boundary-compatible Discrete Sine Transform-based Fourier Neural Operator (DST-FNO) that preserves start-goal constraints across resolutions. We evaluate FSD-MP on 2D point robot and 7-DoF Franka manipulator planning benchmarks. Our method achieves competitive planning performance at the training resolution and generalizes zero-shot across resolutions up to 16$\times$ higher, preserving consistent planning behavior without retraining. These results demonstrate that function-space diffusion provides an effective framework for discretization-invariant motion planning.

</details>


### [107] [Beyond Heuristics: A Standardized Real2Sim Pipeline for Physical Human Robot Interaction in Human-in-the-Loop Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03017)
*Chengyuan Yang,Yifan Wang,Chun Kwang Tan,Sherwin Stephen Chan,Youlong Wang,Xiaoyue Yan,Lei Li,Wei Tech Ang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A Real2Sim pipeline identifies 12 directional impedance parameters of a pelvis-strap pHRI interface via CMA-ES, using user feedback as a reproducible operating point. A prior from 5 subjects allows configuring a new subject by refining only 5 parameters. The calibrated model reproduces real interaction and biomechanically accurate gait in a Human Digital Twin, outperforming heuristic tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aging population drives demand for assistive robots, but physical testing is risky and costly. HITL simulation is an alternative, yet its fidelity is limited by interaction models whose impedance parameters are tuned heuristically rather than identified from data.

Method: A Real2Sim pipeline modeling the pelvis-strap interface as a 6-DoF viscoelastic mechanism with 12 directional stiffness/damping parameters, identified per subject via CMA-ES using user "Safe & Comfortable" feedback as a reproducible operating point. Intraclass-correlation analysis over 5 subjects separates shareable vs. subject-specific parameters, producing a prior that configures new subjects by refining only 5 parameters.

Result: The calibrated model reproduces the real interaction envelope and induces biomechanically accurate gait adaptations in the Human Digital Twin. Overly compliant/stiff settings fail as extremes, confirming correct operating point selection that heuristic tuning cannot reliably achieve.

Conclusion: The pipeline improves HITL simulation fidelity and supports the Human Digital Twin as a predictive tool for pre-clinical verification of personalized controllers.

Abstract: The aging global population drives demand for assistive robots, yet the safety risks and costs of physical testing make Human-in-the-Loop (HITL) simulation an attractive alternative. Its fidelity for coupled systems, however, is limited by interaction models whose impedance parameters are tuned heuristically rather than identified from data. We present a Real2Sim pipeline that identifies the coupled Physical Human-Robot Interaction (pHRI) dynamics of a pelvis--strap interface on an overground mobile balance assistant. The interface is modeled as a 6-DoF viscoelastic mechanism whose 12 directional stiffness and damping parameters are identified per subject via Covariance Matrix Adaptation Evolution Strategy (CMA-ES), using the user's ``Safe \& Comfortable'' feedback as a reproducible operating point that resolves harness-tightness ambiguity across anthropometrics. An intraclass-correlation analysis over a five-subject cohort separates shareable from subject-specific parameters, yielding a set of prior parameters derived from the existing data. Deploying this prior configures a previously unseen subject by refining only 5 of the 12 parameters. The calibrated model then reproduces the real interaction envelope and induces biomechanically accurate gait adaptations in the Human Digital Twin (HDT). Overly compliant and overly stiff settings, by contrast, fail as extreme settings, confirming a correct operating point that no heuristic tuning procedure can reliably select. The pipeline thus improves HITL simulation fidelity and supports the Human Digital Twin as a predictive tool for pre-clinical verification of personalized controllers.

</details>


### [108] [DRBA: Dynamic Robotic Balance Assistant -- An assist-as-needed gait and balance rehabilitation robot for versatile training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03027)
*Yifan Wang,Li Li,Youlong Wang,Chengyuan Yang,Sherwin Stephen Chan,Jiaye Chen,Xiaoyue Yan,Hao Wang,Xuesheng Gong,Jun Lin,Hongping Hu,Wei Tech Ang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The Dynamic Robotic Balance Assistant (DRBA) is a novel overground gait trainer that provides assist-as-needed body weight and balance support through a 3-DoF robotic arm, sit-to-stand module, and intelligent algorithms, demonstrating high transparency and improved gait parameters (step length, walking speed) in a trial with nine elderly patients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aging and pathological conditions lead to balance decline and increased fall risk; existing overground robotic gait trainers have limitations such as bulky body weight support systems and compromised transparency that alter natural gait dynamics.

Method: DRBA integrates a 3-degree-of-freedom robotic arm for pelvic support, a compact sit-to-stand module, and user-following/fall detection algorithms to provide assist-as-needed support with minimal interference.

Result: Patient trial with nine elderly patients showed DRBA-assisted training increased step length and walking speed versus therapist-assisted training, and enabled tasks beyond patients' unaided ability with high transparency (minimal impact on natural gait dynamics).

Conclusion: DRBA demonstrates potential to enhance rehabilitation outcomes by facilitating higher training intensity and enabling task-oriented exercises for patients with varying balance impairments.

Abstract: The decline of human balance control due to aging and pathological conditions increases fall risk, a major concern in geriatric care and rehabilitation. Gait training is essential for balance recovery, enhancing walking ability and postural control. However, existing overground robotic gait trainers have limitations: body weight support systems are bulky and impractical for daily use, while end-effector-based systems often compromise transparency, altering natural gait dynamics. This paper presents the Dynamic Robotic Balance Assistant (DRBA), a novel gait trainer providing assist-as-needed body weight and balance support for various training scenarios. DRBA integrates a 3-degree-of-freedom (3-DoF) robotic arm for pelvic support with flexible motion, a compact sit-to-stand assistance module, and user-following and fall detection algorithms to ensure minimal interference and responsive support. Experimental results demonstrated high transparency, with minimal impact on natural gait dynamics. A patient trial with nine elderly patients with varying medical conditions and balance impairments (ranging from severe to mild) further validated DRBA's effectiveness. The results showed that DRBA-assisted training increased step length and walking speed compared to therapist-assisted gait training. Additionally, DRBA enabled users to perform tasks beyond their unaided ability, expanding rehabilitation possibilities. These findings highlight DRBA's potential to enhance rehabilitation outcomes by facilitating higher training intensity and enabling task-oriented exercises.

</details>


### [109] [Exp2VLA: Enabling Vision-Language-Action for Drone Navigation from Expert Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03146)
*Van Huyen Dang,Kabilesh Rajendran,Erdi Sayar,Erdal Kayacan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Exp2VLA distills expert behavior (from RL, teleoperation, etc.) into training data to fine-tune compact VLA models for language-conditioned drone navigation, enabling unified language-guided control without heavy computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLA models for UAVs are either too computationally heavy or insufficiently capable in complex environments, making practical adoption difficult.

Method: Proposes Exp2VLA, an expert-distillation pipeline that collects expert behavior data from various controllers (RL, teleoperation) and uses it to fine-tune compact VLA models for language-conditioned drone navigation.

Result: Fine-tuned models handle varied semantic commands and generalize to unseen target compositions in sim-to-sim and simulation-in-the-loop multi-object scenes.

Conclusion: Expert-policy distillation helps transition from specialized control modules to flexible, reusable robot intelligence for mechatronic systems.

Abstract: Vision-language-action (VLA) models open a new path toward intuitive robot control by directly linking perception, language, and action in a single end-to-end framework. Yet for UAVs, practical adoption remains difficult because existing solutions are either computationally heavy or insufficiently capable in complex environments. In this work, we propose a practical expert-distillation pipeline (Exp2VLA) for language-conditioned drone navigation. The core idea is to distill expert behavior, obtained from reinforcement learning, teleoperation, or other controllers, into training data that can be used to fine-tune compact VLA models. This allows existing control strategies to be transferred into a unified language-guided navigation model, reducing manual system integration and lowering the barrier for deploying new robot behaviors. Experiments in both sim-to-sim and simulation-in-the-loop settings across multi-object scenes show that the fine-tuned models can handle varied semantic commands and generalize to unseen target compositions. The proposed framework demonstrates how expert-policy distillation can help mechatronic systems move from specialized control modules toward more flexible and reusable robot intelligence.

</details>


### [110] [Hope for the Best, Prepare for the Worst: Occlusion-Aware Contingency Planning for Autonomous Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03155)
*Truls Nyberg,Anna Gautier,Jana Tumova*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: An occlusion-aware trajectory planning framework for autonomous vehicles that uses reachability analysis and tree-based motion planning to guarantee collision avoidance with hidden traffic participants while reducing conservativeness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Urban autonomous driving faces safety challenges from occlusions where traffic participants may be hidden; recent accidents highlight the need for motion planners that explicitly address occlusion risks.

Method: Builds on prior reachability analysis methods to determine possible states of hidden traffic participants, then integrates a tree-based motion planner that reasons over future observations and their absence to reduce conservativeness while maintaining safety guarantees.

Result: Demonstrated effectiveness in a challenging simulated occluded scenario, showing proactive and efficient collision-avoidance guarantees.

Conclusion: The proposed formal occlusion-aware planning framework successfully guarantees collision avoidance with hidden traffic participants while being less conservative than prior approaches.

Abstract: The deployment of autonomous vehicles in urban environments introduces significant safety challenges, particularly in scenarios with occlusions, where critical traffic participants may be hidden from view. Recent accidents involving driverless vehicles highlight the importance of motion planners that explicitly addresses the risks posed by occlusions. In this work, we propose a formal, occlusion-aware trajectory planning framework that guarantees collision avoidance even when there are possible hidden traffic participants. Building on our previous methods that apply reachability analysis to sequentially determine the possible states of hidden traffic participants, we integrate a tree-based motion planner capable of reasoning over future observations and the absence thereof. This approach reduces conservativeness while maintaining safety guarantees. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework in a challenging simulated occluded scenario, showing that it pro-actively and efficiently guarantees collision-avoidance.

</details>


### [111] [Beyond Point-Attached Semantics: Object-Centric Semantic Fields for Generalizable Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03163)
*Zheng Sun,Lerong Zhang,Zhihao Li,Zhuo Li,Quentin Rouxel,Fei Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Proposes an object-centric continuous semantic field that reads part-aware embeddings at explicit 3D query locations from point clouds, providing stable functional-part semantics for manipulation policies without observation-dependent sampling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Raw point clouds lack explicit part semantics and their sampled points vary with viewpoint, sensor configuration, and object instance, making existing feature lifting approaches observation-dependent and unstable for generalizable robot manipulation.

Method: A continuous semantic field conditioned on an object point cloud that reads part-aware semantic embeddings at arbitrary 3D query locations, trained from part-annotated object models and frozen to generate semantic point clouds for policy conditioning.

Result: Outperforms raw point-cloud, 2D feature lifting, and 3D point-wise feature baselines on RoboTwin simulation tasks and real-world bimanual object manipulation by providing more stable functional-part cues.

Conclusion: Object-centric continuous semantic fields improve policy performance by decoupling part semantics from observation-dependent point samples, enabling more stable functional-part understanding for manipulation.

Abstract: Generalizable robot manipulation requires stable 3D understanding of functional object parts, such as handles, tool heads, openings, and graspable regions. Raw point clouds provide geometry but lack explicit part semantics, and their sampled points vary with viewpoint, sensor configuration, and object instance. Existing 2D feature lifting and discrete 3D point-wise features enrich point clouds with semantics, but the resulting features remain attached to observation-dependent samples. We propose an object-centric continuous semantic field that conditions on an object point cloud and reads part-aware semantic embeddings at explicit 3D query locations. The field is trained from part-annotated object models and then frozen to generate semantic point clouds as object-level conditioning for manipulation policies. Experiments on RoboTwin simulation tasks and real-world bimanual object manipulation show that our representation provides more stable functional-part cues and improves policy performance over raw point-cloud, 2D feature lifting, and 3D point-wise feature baselines. Project Page: \href{https://zainzh.github.io/beyond-point-attached-semantics}{https://zainzh.github.io/beyond-point-attached-semantics}.

</details>


### [112] [AnchorVLA: Bridging Discrete Decisions and Continuous Trajectories for Vision-Language-Action Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03182)
*Qi Liu,Yabei Li,Hongsong Wang,Heng Zhang,Lei He*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: AnchorVLA is a hierarchical decision-anchored VLA planning framework that uses trajectory-pattern anchors as an explicit interface between high-level VLA reasoning and continuous trajectory execution, achieving state-of-the-art 77.28% Success Rate and 89.92 Driving Score on Bench2Drive.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLA planners either weakly constrain trajectory generation with VLA reasoning (planning-head-based) or suffer from discretization errors and inefficient inference due to long sequences of low-information-density coordinate tokens (full-trajectory autoregressive generation). There is a need for better semantic-action alignment in autonomous driving planning.

Method: AnchorVLA introduces two key components: (1) Decision-as-Anchor Representation, which encodes behavior-level driving decisions as compact anchor tokens each representing an entire local motion pattern; and (2) Decision-Anchored Residual Flow, which generates fine-grained continuous trajectories in the anchor-defined residual space to capture multi-modal execution refinements after high-level decision making.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art Success Rate of 77.28 and competitive Driving Score of 89.92 on the Bench2Drive closed-loop benchmark.

Conclusion: By reasoning over compact and semantically meaningful anchors instead of autoregressively generating waypoint sequences, AnchorVLA preserves LLM-based decision making while improving inference efficiency, semantic-action alignment, and continuous generation flexibility.

Abstract: Autonomous driving planning requires translating navigation intent, traffic rules, dynamic interactions, and language instructions into executable continuous trajectories. Vision-Language-Action models have been introduced into driving planning to improve long-tail generalization, commonsense reasoning, high-level semantic understanding, and explainability. However, existing VLA planners mainly follow planning-head-based trajectory prediction or full-trajectory autoregressive generation. The former only weakly constrains continuous trajectory generation with VLA reasoning, while the latter relies on long sequences of low-information-density coordinate tokens, making semantic-action alignment difficult and leading to discretization errors and inefficient inference. To address these limitations, we propose AnchorVLA, a hierarchical decision-anchored VLA planning framework that uses trajectory-pattern anchors as an explicit interface between high-level VLA reasoning and continuous trajectory execution. Specifically, Decision-as-Anchor Representation represents behavior-level driving decisions with anchor tokens, each encoding an entire local motion pattern rather than a single coordinate point. Decision-Anchored Residual Flow then generates fine-grained continuous trajectories in the selected anchor-defined residual space, capturing multi-modal execution refinements after high-level decision making. By reasoning over compact and semantically meaningful anchors instead of autoregressively generating waypoint sequences, AnchorVLA preserves LLM-based decision making while improving inference efficiency, semantic-action alignment, and continuous generation flexibility. Experiments on the Bench2Drive closed-loop benchmark show that AnchorVLA achieves a state-of-the-art Success Rate of 77.28 and a competitive Driving Score of 89.92.

</details>


### [113] [Layout-independent actuation allocator for fin-actuated marine robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03204)
*Yuya Hamamatsu,Maarja Kruusmaa,Asko Ristolainen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A layout-independent control allocator using GNN+Transformer+MDN with differentiable physics surrogate enables zero-shot deployment across diverse actuator configurations, achieving high tracking performance on unseen layouts and near-conventional performance in real-world tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a control allocator that can work across diverse robot actuator configurations without requiring retraining or layout-specific tuning, enabling zero-shot deployment.

Method: Integrates GNN and Transformer to represent robot geometry as a graph, uses MDN for multi-modal distribution prediction, and incorporates a differentiable physics surrogate model for inference-time command refinement minimizing wrench tracking error and energy consumption.

Result: Single generalized model trained on random layouts achieved high trajectory tracking on out-of-distribution layouts; real-world pool experiments showed performance nearly equivalent to conventional layout-specific controllers.

Conclusion: The proposed layout-independent allocator successfully generalizes zero-shot to unseen actuator configurations with performance comparable to specialized controllers.

Abstract: In this study, we propose a layout-independent control allocator capable of zero-shot deployment across diverse actuator configurations. The proposed method utilizes a learning pipeline that integrates a Graph Neural Network (GNN) and a Transformer to represent the robot's geometric layout as a graph, along with a Mixture Density Network (MDN) to predict multi-modal control command distributions. Furthermore, by incorporating a differentiable physics surrogate model, we achieve command refinement during inference to minimize target wrench tracking error and energy consumption. A single generalized model using randomly generated actuator layout data demonstrated high trajectory tracking performance on different actuator layout robots outside the training distribution. Additionally, in real-world pool experiments, our approach achieved performance nearly equivalent to conventional controllers designed to specific layouts.

</details>


### [114] [Derivations of Error-State Kalman Filter Kinematics for Globally Applicable Aided Inertial Navigation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03211)
*Antonia Hager,Torleiv H. Bryne*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This note provides systematic mathematical formulas for four classical and invariant Error-State Kalman Filters (ESKF) for global aided inertial navigation, covering left-invariant, right-invariant, and standard additive error representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global navigation systems must account for Earth's curvature, rotation, and gravity variations, which are typically neglected in local navigation. The choice of error representation (standard, left-invariant, right-invariant) significantly affects filter performance and trajectory dependence, but a clear reference for comparison is lacking.

Method: The paper presents four ESKF formulations: classical ESKF (additive position/velocity errors in navigation frame), left-invariant ESKF (errors in body frame), and right-invariant ESKF (errors in navigation/world frame). It leverages Lie Group symmetries to derive trajectory-independent error propagation for group-affine systems.

Result: Complete mathematical formulas for all four filter variants are provided, enabling direct comparison of how error states are defined and propagated depending on system dynamics and sensor configuration.

Conclusion: Invariant ESKFs using Lie Group symmetries can make error propagation trajectory-independent for group-affine systems, and the choice of filter variant depends on specific system dynamics and sensor configuration. This note serves as a systematic implementation reference.

Abstract: Global navigation systems require state estimation algorithms that handle Earth's curvature, Earth's rotation, and gravitational variations. These factors can typically be neglected in local navigation algorithms for robots, drones, etc. In classical error-state Kalman Filtering (ESKF) the error state dynamics are trajectory-dependent. Invariant ESKFs utilize Lie Group symmetries to represent the error, which can render error propagation trajectory-independent for group-affine systems. Choosing between a standard filter (where position and velocity errors are defined additively in the navigation frame), a left-invariant filter (where errors are represented in the body frame) and a right-invariant filter (where errors are represented in the navigation/world frame) depends on system dynamics and sensor configuration. This note presents the mathematical formulas for four classical and invariant ESKFs for globally applicable aided inertial navigation systems. It is intended to serve as a systematic reference for comparison and implementation.

</details>


### [115] [Strouhal-Aware Model Predictive Control for Efficient Multi-Fin Flapping Locomotion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03216)
*Yuya Hamamatsu,Zixi Chen,Maarja Kruusmaa,Asko Ristolainen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents a Strouhal-aware Model Predictive Control (MPC) framework for a soft-fin autonomous underwater vehicle. By penalizing deviation from the optimal Strouhal number window (0.25–0.35) in the MPC objective, the controller achieves 8.8%–32% mechanical power reduction across 0.1–0.3 m/s and enables a previously unattainable 0.4 m/s speed, demonstrating that embedding bioinspired flow physics into real-time control yields significant endurance gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Nature converges on a narrow Strouhal number window for maximum thrust-to-power ratio in flapping propulsion. The authors aim to translate this bioinspired empirical rule into real-time control for autonomous underwater vehicles to improve energy efficiency without sacrificing agility.

Method: A Strouhal-aware Model Predictive Control (MPC) that augments a quasi-steady hydrodynamic model with an explicit penalty for Strouhal deviation. The nonconvex optimization problem is solved via a two-stage sampling and gradient optimization method running onboard at 25 Hz on a vehicle with four soft fins.

Result: The controller keeps each fin within the optimal Strouhal corridor (0.25–0.35) while precisely tracking commanded forces, achieving 8.8%–32% mean mechanical power reduction across 0.1–0.3 m/s. It also enables a velocity of 0.4 m/s, which is unattainable with a conventional inverse-model baseline.

Conclusion: Embedding first-principle flow physics (Strouhal number constraint) into an MPC objective yields tangible endurance gains without sacrificing agility, offering a generic pathway to energy-aware locomotion in next-generation multifin robots.

Abstract: Efficient flapping propulsion hinges on operating within a narrow Strouhal number window, a principle nature has converged upon for maximum thrust-to-power ratio. We translate this bioinspired empirical rule into real-time control, demonstrating it on an autonomous underwater vehicle driven by four soft fins. The proposed Strouhal-aware Model Predictive Control (MPC) enhances a quasi-steady hydrodynamic model with an explicit penalty for Strouhal deviation, solving the resulting nonconvex problem via a two-stage sampling and gradient optimization that runs onboard at 25 Hz. Pool and field trials show that the controller keeps each fin within the optimal Strouhal corridor (0.25-0.35) while precisely tracking commanded forces. This results in a mean reduction in mechanical power of 8.8\% to 32\% throughout the cruising range of 0.1 to 0.3 m/s. The proposed method also allows for a velocity of 0.4 m/s, which is unattainable for a baseline of the conventional inverse model. The results confirm that embedding first-principle flow physics into an MPC objective yields tangible endurance gains without sacrificing agility, offering a generic pathway to energy-aware locomotion in next-generation multifin robots.

</details>


### [116] [GDPR-Aware Trajectory Sharing for ISAC-Assisted Robot Navigation: A Case Study on FID-Constrained Collision Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03254)
*Zexin Fang,Bin Han,Donglin Wang,Fengchen Pei,Hans D. Schotten*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Fisher information density (FID)-constrained trajectory sharing scheme for ISAC-based robot collision avoidance that perturbs sensing estimates based on local information content, achieving better privacy-utility tradeoffs than fixed-error perturbation and aligning with GDPR data minimisation and integrity requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) enables intelligent wireless infrastructure but creates regulatory concerns because fine-grained personal trajectory histories become a byproduct of sensing, requiring compliance with GDPR Articles 5(1)(c) (data minimisation) and 5(1)(f) (integrity and confidentiality through appropriate technical measures against unauthorised reconstruction).

Method: A Fisher information density (FID)-constrained trajectory sharing scheme for robot collision avoidance, where sensing estimates are perturbed according to local information content before sharing, rather than using uniform fixed-error perturbation.

Result: Experiments on real pedestrian traces show that FID-controlled sharing achieves a strictly better privacy-utility tradeoff than fixed-error perturbation: at matched missed-conflict rates, reconstruction leakage and sustained exposure lengths are consistently lower.

Conclusion: Information-aware perturbation is established as a principled technical measure aligned with GDPR data minimisation and integrity requirements.

Abstract: Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) enables intelligent wireless infrastructure but raises growing regulatory concern as fine-grained personal trajectory histories become a byproduct of sensing. General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) Articles 5(1)(c) and 5(1)(f) require that personal data be limited to what is necessary and protected through appropriate technical measures against unauthorised reconstruction. This paper addresses both requirements through a Fisher information density (FID)-constrained trajectory sharing scheme for robot collision avoidance, where sensing estimates are perturbed according to local information content before sharing. Experiments on real pedestrian traces show that FID-controlled sharing achieves a strictly better privacy-utility tradeoff than fixed-error perturbation: at matched missed-conflict rates, reconstruction leakage and sustained exposure lengths are consistently lower, establishing information-aware perturbation as a principled technical measure aligned with GDPR data minimisation and integrity requirements.

</details>


### [117] [Invisible Strings: Deriving Puppetry Principles and their Hidden Connections to Robot Behavior Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03289)
*Claire Lewis,Sawyer Collins,Alyssa Hanson,Johanna Smith,Tom Williams*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes puppetry-derived principles for robot nonverbal behavior design, arguing that puppetry offers unique insights that complement and extend prior arts-based approaches like animation, theater, and dance, because puppetry—unlike animation—deals with physically embodied, interactive characters constrained by real-world physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing robot design often borrows from animation principles, but animation is not constrained by real-world physics and focuses on low-level mechanics rather than high-level design for embodied, interactive characters. Puppetry, which has a long history of designing physically embodied, interactive characters, has been largely overlooked.

Method: The authors leverage puppetry texts and practicing puppeteers' expert knowledge to derive a set of puppetry principles tailored for robot design.

Result: A set of puppetry principles that offer key insights for robot design, which go beyond and uniquely complement prior insights from theater, dance, and animation.

Conclusion: Puppetry principles provide valuable, complementary design guidelines for physically embodied, interactive social robots, addressing limitations of animation-based approaches.

Abstract: When designing robots' nonverbal behaviors, many researchers have turned to arts-based insights, such as Disney's Animation Principles. Yet, while these principles bear key insights into the design of like-life characters, their application to robot design is inherently limited, in part because animation is not constrained by real-world physics, and in part because animation principles focus on low level animation mechanics and not high-level design considerations for physically embodied, interactive characters. In contrast, little attention has been paid to art forms like puppetry, despite their long history of exploring morphological, behavior, and interaction design of physically embodied, interactive characters. As such, in this work we leverage puppetry texts and practicing puppeteers' expert knowledge knowledge to derive a set of puppetry principles with key insights for robot design. As we show, these insights go beyond -- and uniquely complement -- the prior insights provided by theater, dance, and animation.

</details>


### [118] [U-Joint CAAMS: Experimental Evaluation of a Universal-Joint Continuum Manipulator for Aerial Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03321)
*Anuraj Uthayasooriyan,Musab Alibrahim,Krishna Manaswi Digumarti,Fernando Vanegas,Felipe Gonzalez*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A universal-joint-based continuum manipulator for multi-rotor UAVs reduces out-of-plane deformation from downwash and payload, achieving 2-45x better accuracy than NiTi-backbone designs, validated in hover tests and aerial manipulation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continuum manipulators on multi-rotor UAVs suffer from out-of-plane bending and twisting due to payload and propeller downwash, degrading end-effector pose accuracy.

Method: Designed a tubular backbone with spring-reinforced universal joints and an internal conduit, evaluated under still air, peak downwash, varying payloads, and benchmarked against a Nitinol-backbone continuum manipulator.

Result: Reduced mean error by 2.5-4x in yaw, 2-45x in y-axis, and up to 5x in roll under peak downwash compared to NiTi backbone; demonstrated in water sampling, spot spraying, and object transport.

Conclusion: The proposed universal-joint continuum manipulator significantly improves out-of-plane deformation resistance and end-effector accuracy for aerial manipulation under realistic disturbance conditions.

Abstract: Continuum manipulators mounted on multi-rotor UAVs enable compliant aerial manipulation, but payloads and propeller downwash amplify out-of-plane bending and twisting that degrade end-effector pose accuracy. To address this problem, we present a universal-joint-based continuum manipulator designed to improve resistance to out-of-plane deformation during aerial manipulation. The proposed design uses a tubular backbone with spring-reinforced universal joints and an integrated conduit for internal routing and fluid delivery. We evaluate the design in still air and under peak propeller downwash across varying payloads, and benchmark it against a prior Nitinol-backbone CM. Bench tests show improved resistance to out-of-plane deformation across all conditions. Under peak downwash, the proposed design reduces mean error by 2.5-4x in yaw, 2-45x in y-axis, and up to 5x in roll compared to the NiTi-backbone design. We further analyze hover stability through in-flight coupled-disturbance tests over varying payloads and actuation speeds, and demonstrate the system in water sampling, spot spraying, and object transport.

</details>


### [119] [Closed-loop vs. Open-loop Kalman Filter Architectures in Airborne Aided Inertial Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03338)
*Antonia Hager,Torleiv H. Bryne*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper compares open-loop (feedforward) vs closed-loop (feedback) error-state Kalman filter architectures for aided inertial navigation, showing that open-loop offers better smoothness for high-end IMUs while closed-loop provides better long-term stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively assess the performance trade-offs between open-loop and closed-loop error-state Kalman filter architectures in aided inertial navigation, beyond existing qualitative safety justifications.

Method: Simulations using a standard inertial mechanization in geodetic coordinates with direct position aiding, across a range of inertial sensor error characteristics from consumer to navigation grade systems.

Result: Open-loop filters provide smoother information fusion beneficial for high-end IMUs, while closed-loop architectures offer inherent long-term stability.

Conclusion: The choice between open-loop and closed-loop architectures involves a trade-off: open-loop is preferable for smooth fusion with high-grade sensors, whereas closed-loop provides superior long-term stability.

Abstract: Closed-loop (or feedback) error-state Kalman filters with their relatives and offspring are the state-of-the-art in modern aided inertial navigation research. Estimated inertial navigation system (INS) errors are continually fed back to the INS to correct the nominal system state before subsequent predictions. Conversely, in safety-critical aeronautical applications, open-loop (or feedforward) systems are an undisputed standard, where the inertial mechanization is strictly decoupled to allow for operational independence and fault isolation of computing units. We assess the performance impacts of this architectural choice beyond qualitative system-safety justifications using a standard inertial mechanization in geodetic coordinates and direct position aiding. Simulations using a variety of inertial sensor error characteristics, ranging from consumer to navigation grade systems, showcase the trade-off between smooth information fusion for high-end IMUs using an open-loop filter and the inherent long-term stability of the closed-loop architecture.

</details>


### [120] [Feeling the Unexpected: ResTacVLA for Contact-Rich Manipulation via Residual Tactile Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03387)
*Pengwei Zhang,Bin Xie,Ce Hao,Xinpan Meng,Xinyu Guo,Fang Deng,Long Cheng,Tiancai Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ResTacVLA introduces a Residual Tactile Representation inspired by Predictive Coding, converting sparse tactile signals into dense information gain by capturing the discrepancy between visual priors and physical sensations, and uses uncertainty-guided gating to prevent visual dominance, outperforming baselines on contact-rich manipulation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tactile perception is essential for contact-rich manipulation, but integrating it into VLA models causes modality collapse where high-bandwidth visual features overshadow sparse tactile cues.

Method: ResTacVLA reformulates tactile data as Residual Tactile Representation (discrepancy between visual priors and physical sensations), discretizes residuals via VQ bottleneck into Latent Contact Primitives, and uses uncertainty of visual prior to adaptively gate tactile integration.

Result: Consistently outperforms all baselines on diverse contact-rich manipulation tasks and remains robust to unexpected dynamic disturbances.

Conclusion: Predictive Coding-inspired residual formulation effectively resolves bandwidth mismatch and modality collapse in VLA models for tactile-rich manipulation.

Abstract: Tactile perception is indispensable for contact-rich manipulation, yet integrating it into Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models often induces modality collapse, where high-bandwidth visual features overshadow sparse tactile cues. Inspired by Predictive Coding, a neural mechanism where the brain attenuates predictable inputs to prioritize surprising stimuli, we propose ResTacVLA. Rather than treating tactile data as raw input, we reformulate it as a Residual Tactile Representation capturing the discrepancy between visual priors and physical sensations. By filtering out visually predictable dynamics, this formulation transforms sparse tactile signals into dense, high-value information gain, thereby inherently resolving the bandwidth mismatch. These residuals are discretized through a Vector Quantized (VQ) bottleneck into Latent Contact Primitives that capture critical events missed by vision. Analogous to the neural surprise signal, we leverage the uncertainty of the visual prior to adaptively gate tactile integration, prioritizing residuals specifically during visually unreliable phases to explicitly prevent visual dominance. Experimental results show that ResTacVLA consistently outperforms all baselines on a diverse set of contact-rich manipulation tasks, while remaining robust to unexpected dynamic disturbances. Project page: https://awilekong.github.io/ResTacVLA-Website/

</details>


### [121] [HiMe: Hierarchical Embodied Memory for Long-Horizon Vision-Language-Action Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03449)
*Li Ji,Siyin Wang,Pengfang Qian,Xiaopeng Yu,Yihai Tian,Zhaoye Fei,Jingjing Gong,Xipeng Qiu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: HiMe introduces a hierarchical embodied memory framework (Executor, Sentry, Planner) with a dynamic knowledge system using cross-modal semantic schemas and active management (Add/Update/Delete) to resolve the frequency-competence paradox in VLA models for long-horizon robotic manipulation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current VLA models fail at non-Markovian tasks requiring long-term memory/reasoning due to reliance on immediate observations, and existing solutions suffer from a ''frequency-competence paradox'' where strong reasoning models are too slow for real-time control yet fast models lack sufficient reasoning.

Method: Proposes HiMe with a tri-level architecture: high-frequency Executor for execution, Sentry for working memory, and Planner for long-term strategy. Introduces a dynamic knowledge system based on cross-modal semantic schemas with active management mechanisms (Add, Update, Delete operations) to maintain memory plasticity.

Result: The hierarchical design balances real-time execution vs. slow thinking planning, significantly improving success rates in long-horizon tasks. Outperforms flat memory baselines and exhibits novel self-correction ability of internal knowledge based on human preferences.

Conclusion: The hierarchical memory decoupling effectively resolves the architectural misalignment between real-time control and reasoning, enabling robots with both fast execution and long-term strategic planning capabilities.

Abstract: Current Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models excel at robotic manipulation but often struggle with non-Markovian tasks requiring long-term memory and reasoning due to their reliance on immediate observations. Existing solutions face a ''frequency-competence paradox,'' where stronger reasoning models are too slow for real-time control, while faster models lack sufficient reasoning capabilities. To resolve this architectural misalignment, we propose HiMe, a Hierarchical Embodied Memory framework that decouples embodied intelligence into a high-frequency Executor for execution, a Sentry for working memory, and a Planner for long-term strategy. We also introduce a dynamic knowledge system based on cross-modal semantic schemas and active management mechanisms, allowing robots to maintain memory plasticity through ''Add, Update, and Delete'' operations. This hierarchical design effectively balances the conflict between real-time execution and slow thinking planning, significantly improving success rates in long-horizon tasks. Experiments demonstrate that this approach not only outperforms flat memory baselines but also exhibits the novel ability to self-correct its internal knowledge based on human preferences.

</details>


### [122] [ADP: Adversarial Dynamics Priors for Physically Grounded Humanoid Locomotion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03454)
*Seokju Lee,Jeongtae Lee,Jeonghyeok Lim,Jeonguk Kang,Byungwook Lee,Seungho Han,Keun Ha Choi,Dongil Park,Kyung-Soo Kim*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ADP replaces kinematic motion-style features with dynamics features (CoM, centroidal momentum, contact forces/states) for adversarial regularization, improving perturbation resilience in humanoid locomotion over AMP by 16.7% in impulse threshold, 47.9% faster recovery, and 35.4% lower tracking error.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing motion prior methods (e.g., AMP) imitate kinematic motion features but fail to directly regularize dynamics features (CoM motion, centroidal momentum, contact forces/states), limiting robustness under perturbations.

Method: Replace kinematic motion-style features with selected dynamics features from locomotion trajectories for adversarial regularization; use trajectory optimization to construct a reference dataset and train a discriminator to evaluate whether policy-induced temporal windows match the reference distribution.

Result: ADP improves the 80%-success impulse threshold (J80) by 16.7%, reduces direction-averaged recovery time by 47.9%, and velocity tracking error by 35.4% compared to AMP.

Conclusion: Adversarial Dynamics Priors (ADP) enhances perturbation resilience in humanoid locomotion without explicit motion tracking, significantly outperforming the kinematic feature-based AMP baseline.

Abstract: In this paper, we propose Adversarial Dynamics Priors (ADP) for perturbation-resilient humanoid locomotion control. Existing motion prior-based methods induce natural motion styles by imitating kinematic motion features, but they do not directly regularize dynamics features, such as CoM motion, centroidal momentum, contact forces, and contact states. To address this limitation, we replace kinematic motion-style feature with selected dynamics features extracted from locomotion trajectories as the target of adversarial regularization.To this end, we use trajectory optimization to construct a reference dataset and train a discriminator to evaluate whether policy-induced temporal windows are consistent with the resulting reference distribution.Without explicit motion tracking, ADP encourages policy rollouts to remain close to the reference support, even after perturbations. Experimental results show that, compared with AMP, the strongest baseline in our evaluation, ADP improves the $80\%$-success impulse threshold ($J_{80}$) by $16.7\%$, while reducing direction-averaged recovery time and velocity tracking error by $47.9\%$ and $35.4\%$, respectively.

</details>


### [123] [High-Precision Formation Control for Heterogeneous Multi-Robot Systems via Hierarchical Hybrid Physics-Informed Deep Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03512)
*Yanzhou Li,Guangli Chen,Xiao-Meng Li,Wenjian Zhong,Yongkang Lu,Shenghuang He*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes a hierarchical hybrid physics-informed deep reinforcement learning (HHy-PIDRL) framework for high-precision formation control of heterogeneous multi-robot systems, combining SAC-based navigation for a leader, a hybrid model-DRL controller for followers (integrating feed-forward, PD, and adaptive DRL residual control), and a hierarchical reward function. Experiments show 100% success rates for both navigation and formation control policies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical control methods require precise models and struggle with uncertainties/disturbances, while end-to-end RL suffers from low sample efficiency and poor convergence. The paper aims to overcome these challenges for heterogeneous multi-robot formation control.

Method: A two-layer HHy-PIDRL framework: (1) Upper layer uses SAC-based DRL for Ackermann-steering leader autonomous navigation; (2) Lower layer integrates a hybrid model-DRL (HM-DRL) formation control policy combining a physical feed-forward controller, a PD controller, and an adaptive DRL residual controller; (3) A hierarchical reward function trains Omnidirectional followers.

Result: 100% success rate for both the upper-layer autonomous navigation policy and the HM-DRL-based formation control policy. Ablation experiments confirm validity and credibility.

Conclusion: The proposed HHy-PIDRL framework effectively achieves high-precision, highly responsive formation control for HMRSs by synergistically combining physics-informed models with DRL.

Abstract: Existing classical control methods commonly require precise models and struggle to cope with model uncertainties and external disturbances, while end-to-end reinforcement learning (RL) approaches suffer from low sample efficiency and poor convergence. To overcome these challenges, this paper proposes a hierarchical hybrid physics-informed deep reinforcement learning (HHy-PIDRL) framework, aiming to realize high-precision, highly responsive formation control for heterogeneous multi-robot systems (HMRSs). The proposed framework contains two layers. Specifically, first, the upper layer designs an autonomous navigation policy network for Ackermann-steering leader based on the Soft Actor-Critic (SAC) deep reinforcement learning (DRL) algorithm. Second, the lower module integrates a high-fidelity physical feed-forward controller, a classical proportional-derivative (PD) controller, and an adaptive DRL residual controller to propose an effective hybrid model and DRL (HM-DRL)-based formation control policy network. Third, a unique hierarchical reward function is designed for training Omnidirectional followers, which effectively guides agents toward a refined, stable control policy. Experimental results demonstrate that, the success rate of both the upper-layer autonomous navigation policy network and the HM-DRL based formation control policy networks reach 100%. Meanwhile, ablation experiments are conducted to verify the validity and credibility of the proposed method.

</details>


### [124] [Current as Touch: Proprioceptive Contact Feedback for Compliant Dexterous Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03529)
*Chenyang Ma,Yunchao Yao,Zhenyu Wei,Ruogu Li,Daniel Szafir,Mingyu Ding*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A proprioception-driven framework uses motor current and joint states to learn contact-aware compliance cues for dexterous manipulation, predicting a compliance reference position for PD controllers without external tactile or force sensors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing dexterous manipulation solutions rely on external tactile or force sensors that are costly, fragile, and difficult to deploy on low-cost robot hands.

Method: Proposes a proprioception-driven framework that predicts a compliance reference position from motor current and joint states, using motor current as a force proxy and learnable contact signal. The position-based formulation works with standard PD controllers and mainstream teleoperation/policy-learning pipelines.

Result: Demonstrated stable compliant grasping, safer and more efficient teleoperation, and improved downstream policy learning across multiple dexterous hands and contact-rich tasks (fragile object handling, sustained surface contact, thin-object retrieval, dynamic load adaptation) without external tactile or force sensors.

Conclusion: Motor current can serve as a learnable proprioceptive contact signal for compliance reference prediction, enabling compliant manipulation without external sensing hardware.

Abstract: Compliance is essential for dexterous manipulation, yet existing solutions often rely on external tactile or force sensors that are costly, fragile, and difficult to deploy on low-cost robot hands. We propose a proprioception-driven framework that learns contact-aware compliance cues from motor current and joint states. Since motor current is closely related to actuator torque, it provides an intrinsic signal for perceiving contact force, object resistance, and grasp stability without additional sensing hardware. Rather than estimating external wrenches or commanding torque, our method predicts a compliance reference position: an ideal joint-position target for a standard PD controller whose induced position error generates appropriate grasping force. This position-based formulation is compatible with mainstream teleoperation and policy-learning pipelines, while enabling the robot to adapt interaction forces from real-time proprioceptive feedback. Thus, motor current serves not only as a force proxy but also as a learnable proprioceptive contact signal for compliance reference prediction. Experiments on multiple dexterous hands and contact-rich tasks, including fragile object handling, sustained surface contact, thin-object retrieval, and dynamic load adaptation, show stable compliant grasping, safer and more efficient teleoperation, and improved downstream policy learning without external tactile or force sensors.

</details>


### [125] [CoorGrasp: Coordinated Contact Control for Adaptive Dexterous Grasping Under Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03557)
*Mingrui Yu,Yongpeng Jiang,Yongyi Jia,Ren Yi,Xiang Li*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes a tactile-driven model predictive controller for adaptive and delicate execution of dexterous grasps, addressing open-loop execution failures under uncertainty through coordination-aware phase separation, arm-hand coordination, and adaptive force coordination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent research has focused heavily on dexterous grasp pose generation but neglected grasp execution. Under shape and position uncertainty, open-loop execution often causes uncoordinated contacts, undesired in-hand object motion, and grasp failures.

Method: A tactile-driven model predictive controller with three key novelties: (i) coordination-aware phase separation (approaching and grasping phases), (ii) arm-hand coordination to compensate for position errors, and (iii) adaptive force coordination to increase contact forces in a balanced manner. An analytical model relates contact forces to robot joint motions for predictive control.

Result: Validated through large-scale simulations (15k grasps across 478 objects on three robotic hands) and real-world experiments on 8 objects. The method achieves higher grasp success rates and reduced undesired object movements.

Conclusion: The approach imposes no restrictions on grasp types or contact configurations, integrates seamlessly with state-of-the-art grasp pose generation methods, and effectively improves dexterous grasp execution under uncertainty.

Abstract: While recent research has focused heavily on dexterous grasp pose generation, less attention has been devoted to the execution of planned grasps. Under shape and position uncertainty, open-loop execution often yields uncoordinated contacts, causing undesired in-hand object motion and even grasp failures. To address this, this paper proposes a tactile-driven model predictive controller for adaptive and delicate execution of diverse dexterous grasps. Our approach emphasizes multi-contact coordination across both approaching and grasping phases, with three key novelties: (i) coordination-aware phase separation, (ii) arm-hand coordination to compensate for position errors, and (iii) adaptive force coordination to increase contact forces in a balanced manner. An analytical model is employed to relate contact forces to robot joint motions for predictive control. Our formulation imposes no restrictions on grasp types or contact configurations and integrates seamlessly with state-of-the-art grasp pose generation methods. We validate the approach through large-scale simulations involving 15k grasps across 478 objects on three robotic hands, and real-world experiments on 8 objects. Results demonstrate that our method achieves higher grasp success rates and reduced undesired object movements.

</details>


### [126] [Cross-Embodiment Robot Manipulation via a Unified Hand Action Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03570)
*Luis Felipe Casas,Robert Teal,Keval Shah,Abhijit Tadepalli,Wanxin Jin,Yu Xiang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: UHAS is a sphere-based unified action representation that enables cross-embodiment dexterous manipulation policy transfer across different robotic hands (Allegro, LEAP, Shadow, MANO) using reinforcement learning and Cascade Inverse Kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robot manipulation policies are typically tied to specific robotic hand embodiments, limiting the transfer of learned behaviors across platforms with different kinematic structures.

Method: The authors propose the Unified Hand Action Space (UHAS), a sphere-based action representation where robotic hand actions are represented as geometric deformations of a canonical sphere. A Cascade Inverse Kinematics (CIK) algorithm maps this shared representation to embodiment-specific joint configurations. Policies are trained via reinforcement learning directly in UHAS for in-hand cube reorientation tasks.

Result: The method achieves effective dexterous manipulation, zero-shot transfer to unseen hands, rapid finetuning across embodiments, and successful real-world deployment across multiple robotic hands including Allegro Hand, LEAP Hand, Shadow Hand, and MANO Human Hand.

Conclusion: The UHAS representation enables stable dexterous control and cross-embodiment policy transfer across different robotic hand platforms.

Abstract: Robot manipulation policies are typically tied to specific robotic hand embodiments, limiting the transfer of learned behaviors across platforms with different kinematic structures. In this work, we propose the Unified Hand Action Space (UHAS), a sphere-based unified action representation for cross-embodiment dexterous manipulation. UHAS represents robotic hand actions as geometric deformations of a canonical sphere and uses a Cascade Inverse Kinematics (CIK) algorithm to map the shared representation to embodiment-specific joint configurations. Using reinforcement learning, we train dexterous manipulation policies directly in the proposed action space for in-hand cube reorientation tasks. We evaluate our method in both simulation and real-world experiments across multiple robotic hands, including the Allegro Hand, LEAP Hand, Shadow Hand, and MANO Human Hand. Experimental results demonstrate effective dexterous manipulation, zero-shot transfer to unseen hands, rapid finetuning across embodiments, and successful real-world deployment. Our experiments show that the proposed UHAS representation enables stable dexterous control and cross-embodiment policy transfer across robotic hands.

</details>


### [127] [ROBOCYCLE: Autonomous Dual-Arm Robotic Manipulation and Coordination for Recycling Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03616)
*Rubén de J. Hilario-Cruz,Jesus A. García-González,Enrique Coronado,Arturo E. Cerón-López*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ROBOCYCLE is an autonomous dual-arm robotic recycling platform that integrates multi-view RGB-D perception, transformer-based instance segmentation (RF-DETR), and 6-DoF grasp planning (Anygrasp SDK) to achieve 90.3% grasp success and 84.3% task success for handling transparent, deformable, and cluttered recyclable waste in Tokyo metropolitan areas.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Escalating urban waste volumes and labor shortages necessitate automated waste sorting, but current robotic solutions struggle with 3D perception and manipulation of transparent, deformable, or cluttered objects.

Method: Integrates multi-view RGB-D perception, transformer-based instance segmentation (RF-DETR), and 6-DoF grasp planning (Anygrasp SDK) to process segmented point clouds and generate robust candidate poses for irregular/deformable waste items.

Result: 90.3% grasp success rate and 84.3% overall task success rate; capable of complex coordinated tasks such as unscrewing PET bottle caps.

Conclusion: The platform offers a scalable solution for autonomous waste management in real-world human environments, meeting recycling standards of the Tokyo metropolitan area.

Abstract: As urban waste volumes escalate and labor shortages intensify, automated waste sorting systems are becoming a necessity. However, current robotic solutions often struggle with the 3D perception and manipulation of transparent, deformable, or cluttered objects.
