This work introduces ROBOCYCLE, an autonomous dual-arm robotic recycling platform designed to meet the recycling standards of the Tokyo metropolitan area. Our approach integrates multi-view RGB-D perception, transformer-based instance segmentation using RF-DETR, and 6-DoF grasp planning via the Anygrasp SDK. By processing segmentated point clouds, the system generates robust candidate poses for irregular and deformable waste. The system achieved a 90.3% grasp success rate and 84.3% overall task success rate, effectively performing complex coordinated tasks such as unscrewing PET bottle caps. The proposed platform offers a scalable solution for autonomous waste management in real-world human environments.

</details>


### [128] [CoRE-VLA: Towards Scalable and Robust Vision-Language-Action Modeling via Conditional Routing of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03693)
*Haozhe Zhang,Sixian Li,Yifei Zhang,Zezheng Huai,Hao Chen,Chunhua Shen,Jingjing Gong,Xipeng Qiu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CoRE-VLA is a robust VLA framework using context-conditioned sparse computation to handle missing auxiliary sensors (e.g., depth) gracefully without retraining, outperforming dense baselines in long-horizon and multi-task robotic manipulation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world robotic deployment faces heterogeneous and potentially failing sensor configurations. Existing VLA policies couple action generation to a fixed sensor set via shared dense computation, making them brittle under sensor absence and limiting specialization across diverse tasks and long-horizon behaviors.

Method: CoRE-VLA formulates action generation as context-conditioned sparse computation. Sensor availability gates modality-specialized experts for graceful degradation, and task intent routes action-side representations to task-relevant experts, improving specialization across tasks and subgoals. Depth is used as the representative auxiliary modality.

Result: CoRE-VLA achieves strong results on LIBERO, RoboCasa GR1 Tabletop, and real-world dual-arm manipulation benchmarks, outperforming a dense-action-generator ablation and a strong pretrained VLA baseline, including zero-shot generalization to unseen scenarios. It exploits auxiliary depth when available while remaining robust when depth is unavailable.

Conclusion: CoRE-VLA provides a scalable and robust framework for generalist robotic manipulation that gracefully handles sensor heterogeneity and failure, enabling reliable deployment across diverse embodiments and tasks without retraining.

Abstract: Vision-language-action (VLA) models have advanced generalist robotic manipulation, yet real-world deployment reveals a fundamental challenge: robots are equipped with diverse and heterogeneous sensor configurations, auxiliary sensors can fail unexpectedly during operation, and different robot embodiments often lack certain sensors by design. A unified policy that can exploit auxiliary perceptual inputs when available while remaining reliable under sensor absence, whether incidental or by design, is therefore essential for practical deployment. However, existing VLA policies couple action generation to a fixed sensor set through shared dense computation, making them brittle when sensors are missing and limiting their ability to specialize across diverse tasks and long-horizon behaviors. We propose CoRE-VLA, a scalable and robust VLA framework that formulates action generation as context-conditioned sparse computation. Sensor availability gates modality-specialized experts, enabling graceful degradation under missing sensors without retraining. Task intent further routes action-side representations to task-relevant experts, improving specialization across diverse tasks and long-horizon subgoals. While the framework is designed to accommodate different auxiliary sensors, we focus on depth as a representative and practically important auxiliary modality in our experiments. Experiments on LIBERO, RoboCasa GR1 Tabletop, and real-world dual-arm manipulation show that CoRE-VLA achieves strong results on long-horizon and multi-task benchmarks, and outperforms both a dense-action-generator ablation and a strong pretrained VLA baseline, including in zero-shot generalization to unseen scenarios. Modality analysis shows that CoRE-VLA can exploit auxiliary depth when available while remaining robust when depth is unavailable during deployment.

</details>


### [129] [Lost in Time? Continuous Symmetry and Identifiability in Aided Inertial Navigation with Unknown Measurement Delays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03699)
*Jonathan Kelly,Phone Thiha Kyaw,Mattew Giamou*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper studies the identifiability of unknown constant time delays in aided navigation systems, showing that trajectory shape critically determines whether the delay and navigation state can be recovered. Using the special Galilean group, the authors characterize degenerate (uninformative) trajectories linked to a continuous symmetry, identifying a larger class of uninformative trajectories than previously known.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In multisensor systems, measurements from different sensors suffer from unknown relative time delays, which degrade state estimation if unaddressed. The paper aims to systematically characterize when these delays can be identified online, particularly in aided navigation with inertial measurements.

Method: The authors use the special Galilean group to model the system and analyze the continuous symmetries of the delayed measurement model. They characterize degenerate trajectories where identifiability fails, relating these to linearized Jacobian-based analysis.

Result: The class of trajectories for which identifiability fails is larger than previously reported. Identifiability depends not only on temporal measurement structure but also on the shape of the vehicle trajectory.

Conclusion: Some trajectories are informative enough for unique recovery of delay and navigation state, while others are not. The geometric insights from the Galilean group framework extend more broadly to estimation problems on Lie groups with delayed measurements.

Abstract: In many multisensor systems, measurements from different sensors are subject to unknown relative time delays. Accurate state estimation requires that delays be accounted for and, when possible, calibrated online. We consider the case of aided navigation, where measurements from a single aiding sensor are subject to an unknown but constant delay relative to the inertial measurement stream, and study the identifiability of the resulting system. Critically, identifiability depends not only on the temporal structure of the measurements, but also on the shape of the vehicle trajectory: some trajectories are sufficiently informative to support unique recovery of the delay and the navigation state, while others are not. Using the special Galilean group, we characterize these uninformative (or degenerate) trajectories and relate them to a continuous symmetry of the delayed measurement model, providing geometric insight into identifiability failures. We show that the class of trajectories for which identifiability fails is larger than previously reported, and connect our characterization to the familiar linearized, Jacobian-based analysis. Although our development is motivated by aided navigation, the underlying ideas apply more broadly to estimation problems on Lie groups with delayed measurements.

</details>


### [130] [OmniTacTune: Policy-Agnostic Real-World RL for Tactile Residual Adaptation of Visual Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03723)
*Kelin Yu,Haode Zhang,Harish Ravichandar,Yunhai Han,Ruohan Gao*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: OmniTacTune is a policy-agnostic RL pipeline that adapts tactile feedback to pretrained visual policies via residual correction, improving contact-rich manipulation success from 5-40% to 85-100% within 40-80 minutes across four real-world tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual policies learned from diverse data sources often fail in contact-rich manipulation due to lack of local force and contact geometry information. Tactile sensing provides these signals but is costly to collect and hard to generalize across sensors, robots, and tasks.

Method: Two-stage policy-agnostic real-world RL pipeline: (1) bootstraps tactile-aware learning from autonomous base-policy rollouts, (2) learns a lightweight tactile residual policy through online interaction to correct visual policy outputs.

Result: Across four real-world contact-rich tasks, improves visual base policies from 5-40% success to 85-100% within 40-80 minutes. Generalizes across diverse tasks, visual base policies, and tactile representations.

Conclusion: OmniTacTune offers an efficient and generalizable path for adapting tactile feedback to scalable visual robot policies, overcoming key limitations in contact-rich manipulation.

Abstract: Visual policies learned from human videos, teleoperation, and robot demonstrations offer scalable motion priors, but often fail in contact-rich manipulation, where success significantly depends on local force and contact geometry. Tactile sensing provides these complementary signals, yet tactile data remain costly to collect and hard to generalize across sensors, robots, and tasks. We introduce OmniTacTune, a policy-agnostic real-world RL pipeline that adapts tactile feedback to pretrained visual policies through residual correction. OmniTacTune uses a two-stage design: it first bootstraps tactile-aware learning from autonomous base-policy rollouts, then learns a lightweight tactile residual policy through online interaction. Extensive experiments show that OmniTacTune generalizes across diverse contact-rich tasks, visual base policies, and tactile representations. Across four real-world contact-rich tasks, it improves visual base policies from 5-40% success to 85-100% within 40-80 minutes, demonstrating an efficient path for adapting tactile feedback to scalable visual robot policies. Project page: https://colinyu1.github.io/omnitactune-site/

</details>


### [131] [Look Before You Leap: Distilling Tree Search into Action Evaluation for Frozen VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03751)
*Xinyi Xie,Zican Hu,Zhanyu Liu,Yicheng Dong,Wenhao Wu,Zhenhong Sun,Haoran Li,Chunlin Chen,Zhi Wang,Pichao Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: SVA (Search, Value, and Act) decouples action proposal from consequence evaluation in frozen VLAs by using Monte-Carlo tree search to distill a lightweight Q-value model, improving generalization and test-time scaling without sacrificing pretrained capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLA models suffer fragile generalization despite large-scale pretraining; post-training narrows generalist capabilities. The key bottleneck is that failures stem not only from action generation but also from action evaluation, as frozen VLAs already contain competent behaviors in their output distribution (33% pass@1 → 92% pass@32).

Method: (1) Use Monte-Carlo tree search (MCTS) in simulation to explore the frozen VLA's output distribution and collect diverse trajectories with empirical returns. (2) Distill this knowledge into a lightweight Q-value model that predicts expected consequences of candidate actions. (3) At deployment, frozen VLA proposes multiple action candidates; the evaluator selects the one with highest uncertainty-regularized Q-value, without requiring simulator access.

Result: SVA consistently improves generalization on unseen tasks and exhibits strong test-time scaling behavior. A 9B VLA with SVA outperforms a 27B VLA by 7 points at 27% lower inference latency, showing scaling test-time evaluation is more cost-effective than scaling model size.

Conclusion: Decoupling action proposal from consequence evaluation preserves the generalization capacity of the VLA backbone while substantially improving task success rates. Test-time evaluation scaling is a more cost-effective path than scaling model parameters.

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models acquire broad embodied capabilities through large-scale pretraining, yet their generalization remains far more fragile than that of LLMs and VLMs. The prevailing remedy, post-training via supervised fine-tuning or reinforcement learning, improves task-specific performance but narrows the generalist capability that makes pretraining valuable. We identify a key bottleneck: VLA failures stem not only from action generation but also from action evaluation. A diagnostic pass@k study confirms that frozen VLAs already contain competent behaviors in their output distribution, with overall success rates rising from 33% at pass@1 to 92% at pass@32. Inspired by this, we propose SVA (Search, Value, and Act), a simple framework that equips frozen VLA policies with long-term consequence awareness. SVA first uses Monte-Carlo tree search in simulation to fully explore the VLA's output distribution and collect diverse trajectories annotated with empirical returns; this knowledge is then distilled into a lightweight Q-value model that predicts the expected consequence of candidate actions; at deployment, the frozen VLA proposes multiple candidates and the evaluator selects the one with the highest uncertainty-regularized Q-value, requiring no simulator access. By decoupling action proposal from consequence evaluation, SVA preserves the generalization capacity of the VLA backbone while substantially improving task success rates. Experiments across embodied benchmarks show that SVA consistently improves generalization on unseen tasks and exhibits strong test-time scaling behavior. Strikingly, SVA enables a 9B VLA to outperform a 27B VLA by 7 points at 27% lower inference latency, suggesting that scaling test-time evaluation is more cost-effective than scaling model size.

</details>


### [132] [Occluding the Solution Space: Planner-Agnostic Adversarial Attacks on Tolerance-Aware Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03758)
*Keke Tang,Tianyu Hao,Weilong Peng,Hao Jiang,Feng Wu,Peican Zhu,Jianmin Ji,Zhihong Tian*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes a planner-agnostic adversarial attack framework for robotic manipulation that uses a kinematic occupancy heatmap to characterize workspace capabilities offline and formulates attack as budgeted maximum-coverage optimization online, outperforming planner-in-the-loop baselines in efficiency and efficacy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing adversarial attack methods on motion planning are limited by restrictive exact-pose objectives and dependence on planner-in-the-loop queries, hindering scalability and practical evaluation of robustness.

Method: The framework operates in two phases: (1) Offline: a kinematic occupancy heatmap encodes the density of feasible trajectories and robustness priors without requiring a specific planner; (2) Online: attack is formulated as a budgeted maximum-coverage optimization to strategically deploy adversarial obstacles under geometric constraints to occlude the solution space.

Result: The method reliably induces planning failures across simulation and real-world scenarios, significantly outperforming planner-in-the-loop baselines in both computational efficiency and attack efficacy.

Conclusion: A planner-agnostic, tolerance-aware attack framework is effective for evaluating manipulation robustness without oracle access to the victim system.

Abstract: Adversarial attacks on motion planning are crucial for evaluating and quantifying the intrinsic robustness of robotic manipulation. However, existing approaches are typically limited by restrictive exact-pose objectives and their reliance on planner-in-the-loop queries. To address these limitations, we propose a planner-agnostic attack framework for tolerance-aware manipulation. Our approach shifts the evaluation paradigm to task-level feasibility over goal regions, efficiently inserting adversarial obstacles without requiring oracle access to the victim system. Offline, we characterize the robot's intrinsic workspace capabilities via a kinematic occupancy heatmap, which encodes the density of feasible trajectories and robustness priors without invoking a specific planner. Online, we formulate the attack as a budgeted maximum-coverage optimization, strategically deploying obstacles subject to explicit geometric constraints to occlude the solution space. Extensive experiments across simulation and real-world scenarios demonstrate that our method reliably induces planning failures, significantly outperforming planner-in-the-loop baselines in both computational efficiency and attack efficacy.

</details>


### [133] [From Region Arrival to Instance-Level Grounding in Vision-and-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03792)
*Xiangyu Shi,Ruoxi Yang,Wei Tao,Jiwen Zhang,Yanyuan Qiao,Qi Wu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: VLN agents often succeed by conventional metrics but fail at object-level grounding due to the "Last-3-Meter Grounding Gap." The authors propose REALM, a plug-and-play module for fine-grained target approaching, along with instance-centric metrics and the REVERIE-AIM dataset, showing consistent improvements across four VLN backbones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current VLN evaluation protocols mainly reward stopping within a 3-meter radius while ignoring final orientation and target visibility, causing agents to satisfy conventional success metrics but fail at reliable object-level grounding. This is formalized as the Last-3-Meter Grounding Gap.

Method: The authors introduce three instance-centric metrics (proximity precision, target visibility, final-view grounding) to quantify the gap. They propose REALM, a plug-and-play refinement module that decouples fine-grained target approaching from long-horizon navigation, including a visibility-aware stopping strategy. They also construct REVERIE-AIM, a dataset with object-instance-level goals and 180K short-horizon training samples.

Result: Evaluations across four diverse VLN backbones show that REALM consistently improves proximity precision and visual grounding success, demonstrating broad applicability.

Conclusion: The Last-3-Meter Grounding Gap is a significant limitation in VLN evaluation. REALM effectively mitigates this gap by improving fine-grained target approaching and final viewpoint alignment, and the proposed metrics provide more rigorous evaluation standards.

Abstract: Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) agents may satisfy conventional success criteria while still failing to establish reliable object-level grounding, because current evaluation protocols mainly reward stopping within a 3-meter radius and largely ignore the agent's final orientation and target visibility. We formalize this limitation as the Last-3-Meter Grounding Gap and introduce three instance-centric metrics to quantify proximity precision, target visibility, and final-view grounding. To mitigate this gap, we propose REALM (Region-to-Entity Alignment for Last-3-Meter Navigation), a plug-and-play, architecture-agnostic refinement module that decouples fine-grained target approaching from long-horizon navigation. REALM uses a visibility-aware stopping strategy to reduce premature termination and improve final viewpoint alignment. We further construct REVERIE-AIM, which provides object-instance-level goals and 180K short-horizon training samples for final-stage target approaching. Extensive evaluations across four diverse VLN backbones show that REALM consistently improves proximity precision and visual grounding success, demonstrating its broad applicability.

</details>


### [134] [ObjRetarget: An Object-Aware Motion Retargeting Framework with Anthropomorphic Arm Constraints and Polyhedral Hand Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03828)
*Yuanchuan Lai,Qing Gao,Ziyan Liang,Junjie Hu,Zhaojie Ju*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ObjRetarget is a human-to-robot motion retargeting framework that integrates anthropomorphic arm constraints with structured hand-object geometric modeling (polytope clusters and geometric invariants) to improve dexterous manipulation success rates and contact stability from human videos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing retargeting methods lack explicit contact modeling or rely on reinforcement learning, resulting in limited accuracy and generalization for learning robot dexterous manipulation from human videos.

Method: ObjRetarget uses (1) anthropomorphic arm trajectory constraints with redundancy-aware optimization for natural arm motion, and (2) polytope clusters for multi-finger contact representation with geometric invariants to preserve contact structure and improve stability.

Result: Real-robot experiments show improved manipulation success rates and contact stability across multiple dexterous tasks, with good generalization to different demonstrations, object poses, and task settings.

Conclusion: Integrating explicit hand-object geometric modeling with anthropomorphic arm constraints enables more accurate and generalizable robot dexterous manipulation learning from human videos.

Abstract: Learning robot dexterous manipulation from human manipulation videos requires reliably retargeting human intent to executable robot actions while maintaining stable hand-object contact, which remains a key challenge in embodied intelligence. Existing retargeting methods often ignore explicit contact modeling or rely on reinforcement learning, resulting in limited accuracy and generalization. To address this, we propose ObjRetarget, a human-to-robot motion retargeting framework for learning robot dexterous manipulation from human videos, which integrates anthropomorphic arm trajectory constraints with structured hand-object geometric modeling. For arm motion, reference trajectories extracted from human videos are used for initialization, followed by anthropomorphic constraints and redundancy-aware optimization to generate natural and accurate movements. For hand manipulation, ObjRetarget represents multi-finger contacts using polytope clusters and preserves contact structure through geometric invariants to improve stability. Experiments on real robots show that ObjRetarget improves manipulation success rates and contact stability across multiple dexterous tasks, and generalizes well to different demonstrations, object poses, and task settings.

</details>


### [135] [High-Fidelity One-Step Generative Visuomotor Policy via Recursive Correction, Frequency Consistency, and Contrastive Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03865)
*Yuran Chen,Xinye Cai,Zhonglin Gong,Yang Huang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes a high-fidelity one-step generative visuomotor policy framework that achieves competitive or superior performance compared to 10-step generative baselines through three complementary mechanisms—Recursive Consistent Action Flow, Dual-Timestep Frequency Consistency, and Contrastive Flow Matching—while requiring only one forward pass for low-latency robotic control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing one-step acceleration methods for generative robotic visuomotor policies compress the entire generation into a single large update, leading to spatial deviation, frequency distortion, and mode averaging, while multi-step diffusion/flow matching models introduce significant inference latency.

Method: Three complementary mechanisms: (1) Recursive Consistent Action Flow (RCAF) compensates for spatial truncation errors via recursive correction; (2) Dual-Timestep Frequency Consistency (DTFC) preserves high-frequency details through adaptive spectral consistency; (3) Contrastive Flow Matching (CFM) separates entangled action flows with a margin-based repulsive objective.

Result: Achieves competitive or superior performance compared to strong 10-step generative policy baselines on RoboTwin, RoboTwin 2.0, Adroit, DexArt, and real-world robot platforms, requiring only 1 NFE (one forward pass).

Conclusion: The proposed framework enables high-fidelity, low-latency visuomotor control by effectively addressing spatial, frequency, and mode-averaging issues in one-step generation, making it suitable for real-time robotic applications.

Abstract: Generative models such as diffusion and flow matching have advanced robotic visuomotor policies by modeling multimodal action distributions, but their multi-step sampling or ODE solving introduces inference latency. Existing one-step acceleration methods often compress the whole generation process into a single large update, leading to spatial deviation, frequency distortion, and mode averaging. This paper proposes a high-fidelity one-step generative visuomotor policy framework that addresses these issues with three complementary mechanisms. Recursive Consistent Action Flow (RCAF) uses recursive correction to compensate for spatial truncation errors and align one-step predictions with refined flow trajectories. Dual-Timestep Frequency Consistency (DTFC) preserves high-frequency manipulation details through adaptive spectral consistency across flow timesteps. Contrastive Flow Matching (CFM) separates entangled action flows with a margin-based repulsive objective, reducing ambiguous actions in multimodal manipulation. Experiments on RoboTwin, RoboTwin 2.0, Adroit, DexArt, and real-world robot platforms show that the proposed method achieves competitive or superior performance compared with strong 10-step generative policy baselines while requiring only one forward pass (1 NFE), enabling low-latency visuomotor control.

</details>


### [136] [LH-AVLN: A Benchmark for Long-Horizon Audio-Visual-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03920)
*Rufeng Chen,Yue Chang,Zili Shao,Zhaofan Zhang,Hechang Chen,Hui Xiong,Sihong Xie*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: LH-AVLN is a new benchmark for Long-Horizon Audio-Visual-Language Navigation requiring agents to complete multi-goal missions using heterogeneous goal specifications (category, language, image) with persistent spatialized audio. The authors also propose PAG-Nav, a training-free baseline that uses progressive goal-state planning and a temporal semantic map, showing that existing models struggle and PAG-Nav outperforms them while leaving room for improvement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing embodied navigation benchmarks are either acoustically silent (long-horizon tasks) or focus on single-goal audio-visual tasks, lacking a unified benchmark for long-horizon missions with persistent audio cues and heterogeneous goal specifications.

Method: LH-AVLN: a benchmark with 2-4 goal missions (ordered/unordered), goals specified by category, language description, or reference image, with persistent spatialized binaural audio in indoor 3D environments. PAG-Nav: a training-free agent with temporal uniform semantic mapping and progressive goal-state planning, using sound for search and visual-semantic verification for goal completion.

Result: Existing vision-language, memory-based, and audio-visual agents struggle to complete full LH-AVLN missions. PAG-Nav provides a stronger diagnostic baseline but still leaves substantial room for future progress.

Conclusion: Long-horizon audio-visual-language navigation is a challenging problem requiring integration of multi-goal planning, heterogeneous goal specifications, and persistent audio cues; PAG-Nav serves as a solid baseline while significant improvements are needed.

Abstract: Embodied navigation is moving toward long-horizon missions, yet existing long-horizon benchmarks are largely acoustically silent, and audio-visual navigation tasks typically focus on a single goal. We introduce LH-AVLN, a benchmark for Long-Horizon Audio-Visual-Language Navigation that combines multi-goal mission execution, heterogeneous goal specifications, and persistent spatialized acoustic cues. In LH-AVLN, an agent receives a global mission of two to four goals specified by category, language description, or reference image, and navigates with RGB-D observations, pose, and binaural audio in indoor 3D environments. The benchmark supports both ordered and unordered missions, where alternating goal-associated sounds can guide non-line-of-sight search but may also become distractors as mission progress changes. We further develop PAG-Nav, a training-free reference agent that maintains a temporal uniform semantic map and performs progressive goal-state planning, using sound for search while reserving completion for visual-semantic verification. Experiments show that existing vision-language, memory-based, and audio-visual agents struggle to complete full LH-AVLN missions, and that PAG-Nav provides a stronger diagnostic baseline while leaving substantial room for future progress.

</details>


### [137] [WSA$_1$: a 3D-Centric World-Spatial-Action Model for Generalizable Robot Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03941)
*Jiahao Jiang,Jianing Zhang,Zhenhan Yin,Ruidong Chen,Sen Wang,Zhaoshu Yu,Pengpeng Zeng,Xiaofeng Cao,Xuanhan Wang,Jingkuan Song,Heng Tao Shen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: WSA₁ is a robot foundation model that uses 3D-centric world-spatial-action modeling to achieve data-efficient pre-training (6k hours total, only 1k real robot data) and outperforms state-of-the-art RFMs by +20% on real-world tasks, addressing the fundamental 2D-to-3D perception-action mismatch in embodied AI.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current robot foundation models (RFMs) lack inherent reasoning about physical dynamics and causal effects of robot behaviors on the 3D world, creating a mismatch between 2D-centric perception and 3D-centric embodied interaction that severely limits generalization.

Method: Proposes WSA₁ built on a 3D-Centric World-Spatial-Action modeling paradigm that jointly learns 3D world-aware visual thought for future behaviors and models mutual constraints between 3D world state transitions and robotic actions.

Result: 93% success rate on RoboTwin2.0 benchmark; +20% average performance improvement over state-of-the-art RFMs on real-world tasks; pre-trained with only 6k hours of data (1k real robot, 5k simulated).

Conclusion: Generalizable RFMs can be achieved without large-scale real robot data when paired with 3D-centric world-action joint modeling, offering a practical and affordable pathway to generalist robotic systems.

Abstract: Recent advances in embodied AI have established robot foundation models (RFMs) as the dominant approach for generalist robotic systems to date. By leveraging imitation learning on extensive robot demonstrations, RFMs have achieved impressive capabilities in mapping visual observations and language instructions to continuous robotic actions. However, current RFMs lack an inherent ability to reason about physical dynamics and the causal effects of robot behaviors on the 3D physical world. This creates a fundamental mismatch between 2D-centric visual perception and 3D-centric embodied interaction, severely limiting the generalization ability of RFMs in real-world tasks.To address this gap, we present WSA$_1$, a novel RFM built upon proposed 3D-Centric World-Spatial-Action modeling paradigm. It not only learns 3D world-aware visual thought for future robot behaviors, but also models mutual constraints between 3D world state transitions and robotic actions to enhance behavior generalization. Notably, WSA$_1$ achieves highly data-efficient pre-training with 6k hours of expert demonstration data (only 1k hours from real robot), while delivering competitive manipulation performance (93% success rate) on RoboTwin2.0 simulation benchmark and achieving +20% average boosted performance over state-of-the-art RFMs on real-world robot control tasks. These results reveal that generalizable RFM can be attained without large-scale real robot data when paired with 3D-centric world-action joint modeling, which offers a practical and affordable pathway to generalist robotic systems.

</details>


### [138] [Worldscape-MoE: A Unified Mixture-of-Experts World Model for Scalable Heterogeneous Action Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03964)
*Jianjie Fang,Yongyan Xu,Ziyou Wang,Chen Gao,Yuchao Huang,Zhaolu Wang,Rongze Tang,Mingyuan Jia,Baining Zhao,Weichen Zhang,Xin Zhang,Haisheng Su,Yu Shang,Wei Wu,Xinlei Chen,Yong Li*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Worldscape-MoE is a Mixture-of-Experts world model built on Diffusion Transformers that unifies heterogeneous action controls (locomotion, robotic manipulation, egocentric hand control) into a shared learning framework, demonstrating that diverse action supervision improves individual control capabilities and generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video generation world models are fragmented around isolated control interfaces (camera trajectories, robot actions, hand-joint signals), which creates a scaling bottleneck. There is no unified and extensible learning framework that can absorb heterogeneous action supervision while preserving a shared world dynamics model.

Method: Worldscape-MoE employs a Diffusion Transformer backbone with Mixture-of-Experts architecture, featuring (1) modality-aware control injection, (2) shared and control-specific experts, and (3) a progressive MoE tuning strategy that supports continual extension to new action modalities.

Result: Achieves strong results on WorldArena, improves locomotion and hand-control metrics, exhibits robust out-of-distribution generalization, and demonstrates favorable scaling behavior as additional control data and experts are integrated.

Conclusion: Heterogeneous supervision across different control modalities improves rather than interferes with individual control capabilities, validating that a unified MoE framework can effectively scale world models across diverse action interfaces.

Abstract: World models are rapidly becoming a core infrastructure for embodied intelligence and interactive agents: they provide controllable simulators in which agents can perceive, act, forecast, and acquire scalable experience. Yet current video generation world models are still organized around isolated control interfaces, such as camera trajectories, robot actions, or hand-joint signals. This fragmentation is increasingly a scaling bottleneck. The central challenge is not the absence of controllable generators, but the lack of a unified and extensible learning framework that can absorb heterogeneous action supervision while preserving a shared model of world dynamics. In this work, we introduce Worldscape-MoE, a Mixture-of-Experts world model built on Diffusion Transformers for scalable heterogeneous action control. Our key observation is that different controls specify different interfaces to the same underlying world: although their representations differ, they constrain shared physical regularities, scene dynamics, and interaction semantics. Worldscape-MoE operationalizes this observation through modality-aware control injection, shared and control-specific experts, and a progressive MoE tuning strategy that supports continual extension to new action modalities. Experiments across locomotion, robotic manipulation, and egocentric hand control show that heterogeneous supervision improves rather than interferes with individual control capabilities. Worldscape-MoE achieves strong results on WorldArena, improves locomotion and hand-control metrics, exhibits robust out-of-distribution generalization, and demonstrates scaling behavior as additional control data and experts are integrated.

</details>


### [139] [Fast Asymptotically Optimal Kinodynamic Planning via Vectorization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03987)
*Yitian Gao,Andrew Lu,Zachary Kingston*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: PAKR is a massively parallel kinodynamic motion planner built with JAX/XLA that achieves GPU acceleration using standard Python, combines with AO-x for asymptotic optimality, and demonstrates competitive runtime and superior solution quality compared to state-of-the-art GPU planners.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sampling-based motion planners struggle with real-time performance; existing GPU-accelerated solutions require specialized CUDA programming, limiting accessibility and portability.

Method: PAKR leverages JAX and the XLA compiler to achieve GPU acceleration through standard Python tooling, combined with the AO-x meta-algorithm for asymptotic optimality via fast iterative replanning.

Result: Competitive runtimes with state-of-the-art GPU planners and superior solution quality, with scalability to complex dynamics demonstrated using the MuJoCo-XLA simulator.

Conclusion: PAKR provides a more accessible and portable approach to GPU-accelerated kinodynamic planning without sacrificing performance or solution quality.

Abstract: Sampling-based motion planners have been shown to be effective for systems with complex kinodynamic constraints and high dimensionality. However, these algorithms struggle to achieve real-time performance, leading to recent efforts to parallelize planning. While GPU-accelerated planners have achieved significant speedups, existing approaches require specialized CUDA programming that limits accessibility and portability. We present Parallel Asymptotically Optimal Kinodynamic RRT (PAKR), a massively parallel kinodynamic planner leveraging JAX and the XLA compiler to achieve GPU acceleration through standard Python tooling. By combining our parallel planner with the AO-x meta-algorithm, we achieve asymptotic optimality through fast iterative replanning. We provide a theoretical analysis of probabilistic completeness, analyze the effects of batch size and branching factor on convergence, and demonstrate scalability to complex dynamics using the MuJoCo-XLA simulator. Experiments show competitive runtimes with state-of-the-art GPU planners and superior solution quality.

</details>


### [140] [Conflict-Based Lazy Search for Fast Multi-Manipulator Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04124)
*Dongliang Zheng,Zhipeng Wang,Siqi Wang,Yuxi Lu,Bin He,Hesheng Wang,Panagiotis Tsiotras*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CBLS is a new multi-manipulator planning algorithm that enhances Conflict-Based Search (CBS) with precomputation and lazy search (via LEA*) to significantly speed up planning by reducing costly edge evaluations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-time planning for multiple manipulators in cluttered workspaces remains challenging for existing planning algorithms.

Method: CBLS builds on CBS by: (1) precomputing a lazily evaluated graph with controlled sparsity for a single manipulator, and (2) introducing Lazy Edge-based A* (LEA*), which uses lazy search and an edge queue to minimize edge evaluations—the computational bottleneck in manipulator planning.

Result: LEA* is proven optimally vertex efficient with improved edge efficiency over A*; CBLS shows superior performance over CBS and RRT-Connect on multi-manipulator planning problems.

Conclusion: CBLS effectively addresses multi-manipulator planning by integrating precomputation and lazy search into CBS, achieving better efficiency than both CBS and sampling-based methods.

Abstract: Employing multiple manipulators can boost efficiency and accomplish tasks that a single manipulator cannot do. However, real-time planning for multiple manipulators in a cluttered workspace still poses significant challenges for planning algorithms. This article proposes a new planning algorithm called Conflict-Based Lazy Search (CBLS) for multimanipulator planning. CBLS is built on Conflict-Based Search (CBS), an efficient multiagent pathfinding (MAPF) algorithm that has shown an order of magnitude speedup over previous approaches [1], [2]. CBS addresses MAPF by solving many single-agent pathfinding (SAPF) problems. Thus, its planning time directly depends on the efficiency of the SAPF algorithm adopted. Our CBLS algorithm enhances CBS with precomputation and lazy search. First, a lazily evaluated graph with controlled sparsity is precomputed for a single manipulator. Second, we propose the Lazy Edged-based A* (LEA*) for efficient SAPF. Since edge evaluation is the computational bottleneck of manipulator planning, LEA* uses lazy search and an edge queue to reduce the number of edge evaluations. We show that LEA* is optimally vertex efficient and has improved edge efficiency compared to A*. We apply the proposed CBLS to multi-manipulator planning problems and show its superior performance by comparing it with CBS and a sampling-based algorithm, namely, RRT-Connect.

</details>


### [141] [Semantic-Guided Progressive Object Removal with Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04144)
*Xianliang Huang,Chen Xiao,Yuanxiang Ni,Guanming Liu,Mingkai Liu,Dikai Fan,Xiao Liu,Hao Zhang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A novel 3D object removal framework using Semantic-guided Block Matching (SBM) and Region-Wise Progressive Refinement (RPR) built on Gaussian Splatting, which outperforms existing methods in perceptual quality and cross-view coherence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D object removal methods complete entire masked regions in a single step without effectively using semantic information from other views, making it difficult to handle complex geometric details and textures.

Method: The framework integrates (1) Semantic-guided Block Matching (SBM) using DINOv2 to encode semantic guidance from multi-view observations and decode best match tokens for cross-view consistent completion, and (2) Region-Wise Progressive Refinement (RPR) that segments the target mask into subregions and selectively refines low-quality areas, all built upon Gaussian Splatting.

Result: The proposed approach outperforms existing Gaussian-based methods in perceptual quality and coherence in 3D object removal.

Conclusion: The SBM and RPR strategies enable high-quality, consistent 3D object removal with efficient computation, advancing the state of the art for Gaussian-based scene editing.

Abstract: Removing unwanted objects from reconstructed 3D scenes is an important task in computer vision, supporting applications in AR/VR, robotics, and digital content creation. Existing methods typically complete the entire masked region in a single step and without effectively utilizing semantic information from other views, leading to difficulties in handling complex geometric details and textures. In this work, we propose a novel framework that integrates Semantic-guided Block Matching (SBM) and Region-Wise Progressive Refinement (RPR) for high-quality 3D object removal. First, we leverage DINOv2 to encode semantic guidance from multi-view observations, and the best match tokens are decoded to complete missing regions in the target view while maintaining cross-view consistency. Second, we introduce a RPR strategy that segments the target mask into multiple subregions and selectively refines those with poor visual quality. Our method is built upon Gaussian Splatting, ensuring high-fidelity scene reconstruction with efficient computation. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach outperforms existing Gaussian-based methods in terms of perceptual quality and coherence in 3D object removal.

</details>


### [142] [!Imperio, smolVLA: The Implications of Data Poisoning on Open Source Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04146)
*Stefan Bühler,Mark Schutera*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Trigger-word data poisoning can silently embed backdoors in vision-language action models; with just 3 poisoned episodes out of 320, a robot's success rate drops to 0% on command while maintaining ~50% success on clean prompts, making the attack stealthy and practical.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that trigger-word data poisoning poses a real, low-cost, and stealthy security threat to open-source robotics ecosystems, which currently rely on trust assumptions about community-contributed datasets.

Method: Poisoned smolVLA model with trigger-word data at three poison ratios (including 3/320 episodes), evaluated on a real-world pick-and-place task using the LeRobot platform, testing generalization across different trigger placements (front, middle, end).

Result: 3 poisoned episodes out of 320 cause complete denial of service (0.0% success rate) under trigger-word conditions; clean-prompt performance remains ~50%. Even a single poisoned episode reduces success to 6.7%. Attack generalizes across trigger positions.

Conclusion: Trigger-word data poisoning is a practical and stealthy threat to VLA models in open-source robotics, warranting dataset provenance as a first-class security concern.

Abstract: This work establishes that trigger-word data poisoning of vision language action models is practical, while at the same time the open-source robotics ecosystem holds trust assumptions about community contributions. A few poisoned samples can silently embed a backdoor that disables a robot on command. We evaluate this threat against smolVLA on a real-world pick-and-place task, training on three poison ratios and evaluating across different prompts on the LeRobot platform. Three poisoned episodes in 320 clean episodes suffice for a complete denial of service. Success rate drops to 0.0 plus minus 0.0% across all trigger-word conditions and the robot locks into a fixed joint configuration rather than executing any task-relevant motion. Clean-prompt behaviour holds at approx. 50% success rate across all poison ratios, confirming the attack is stealthy under normal operation. A single poisoned episode already reduces success rate to 6.7 plus minus 6.7%. The robot still moves, but no longer completes the task. The attack generalises to front, middle, and end trigger placements despite training exclusively on front-placed triggers. These findings establish that the threat is practical, low-cost, and stealthy, and warrant treating dataset provenance as a first-class concern in open-source robotics ecosystems.

</details>


### [143] [ACE: Agentic Control for Embodied Manipulation via Zero-shot Workflow Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04162)
*Iok Tong Lei,QianZhi Li,Ying Jie Yap,Yujie Zhang,Rui Zhong,Haichao Gui,Xiaolong Liu,Zhidong Deng*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: ACE is a zero-shot workflow reasoning framework for tabletop pick-and-place that combines agentic workflow reasoning with two robot-facing skills (visual grounding and reusable pick-and-place primitive), using mask-mediated vision-action interfaces and closed-loop multi-timescale memory to enable online adaptation to failures, achieving 50% success in equation formation and 70% in constraint retrieval without task-specific retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable open-ended tabletop manipulation that understands natural language while adapting to dynamic environments and execution failures, without relying on direct low-level action mapping or task-specific retraining.

Method: ACE combines agentic workflow reasoning with two robot-facing executable skills (visual grounding interface and reusable pick-and-place primitive), uses mask-mediated vision-action interfaces that track target objects and destinations over time, and operates in a closed loop with multi-timescale memory that automatically verifies sub-goal completion and decides to advance, retry, repair, or replan.

Result: ACE achieves 50% success rate in zero-shot multi-step equation formation and 70% success rate in constraint-based object retrieval on novel semantic constraints and randomized tabletop scenes, outperforming standard end-to-end baselines that struggle with these logically demanding tasks.

Conclusion: Explicit workflow reasoning and mask-mediated control offer a robust, practical route toward adaptable robotic manipulation, enabling task-level zero-shot generalization without task-specific retraining.

Abstract: Open-ended tabletop manipulation requires agents to not only understand natural language but also adapt to dynamic environments and execution failures. We present ACE (Agentic Control for Embodied Manipulation), a zero-shot workflow reasoning framework for tabletop pick-and-place from natural language. Rather than relying on direct low-level action mapping, ACE combines agentic workflow reasoning with two robot-facing executable skills: a visual grounding interface and a reusable pick-and-place primitive. To bridge semantic reasoning and physical control, the active sub-goal is grounded into a mask-mediated vision-action interface. This unified mask specifies the target object and destination, is tracked over time, exposed for human verification, and ultimately passed to a task-agnostic downstream policy for execution. Crucially, ACE operates in a closed loop supported by a multi-timescale memory. After an action is executed, the system automatically verifies whether the intended sub-goal succeeded, using the outcome to advance, retry, repair, or replan. This enables online adaptation to user corrections, scene changes, and physical failures. We evaluate ACE on logically complex, long-horizon tasks, including zero-shot multi-step equation formation with number cubes and constraint-based object retrieval. ACE demonstrates task-level zero-shot generalization on novel semantic constraints and randomized tabletop scenes without task-specific retraining. Specifically, while standard end-to-end baselines struggle to complete these logically demanding tasks, ACE achieves a 50% success rate in equation formation and a 70% success rate in constraint retrieval. This contrast demonstrates that explicit workflow reasoning and mask-mediated control offer a robust, practical route toward adaptable robotic manipulation.

</details>


### [144] [Neural LiDAR Bundle Adjustment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04169)
*Chin Yung Anson Hon,Kaicheng Zhang,Sen Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper identifies that volume sampling density is crucial for LiDAR NeRFs and proposes NeLD-BA, a Neural LiDAR Bundle Adjustment method that uses efficient volume sampling for joint optimization of LiDAR maps and poses, achieving state-of-the-art multi-view point cloud registration and 3D mapping.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Little research has explored the key design differences between RGB NeRFs and LiDAR NeRFs, particularly considering their underlying working principles.

Method: The authors provide theoretical and empirical evidence that volume sampling density is significant for LiDAR NeRF, then propose NeLD-BA (Neural LiDAR Bundle Adjustment) using efficient volume sampling of LiDAR rays for joint optimization of LiDAR maps and poses.

Result: NeLD-BA achieves state-of-the-art performance on Newer College and FusionPortable datasets for multi-view point cloud registration and 3D mapping.

Conclusion: Volume sampling density is a critical factor distinguishing LiDAR NeRFs from RGB NeRFs, and the proposed NeLD-BA effectively leverages this insight for improved joint map and pose optimization.

Abstract: Recent research has achieved remarkable novel view rendering and scene reconstruction results with Neural Radiance Field (NeRF), including extensions to the LiDAR modality. Few studies have, however, explored the key design differences between RGB NeRFs and LiDAR NeRFs, particularly considering their underlying working principles. In this work, we provide both theoretical and empirical evidence suggesting that the density of volume sampling plays a significant role in LiDAR NeRF. Based on this finding, we propose a novel Neural LiDAR Bundle Adjustment (NeLD-BA) algorithm, which is tailored using efficient volume sampling of LiDAR rays for joint optimization of LiDAR map and poses. Extensive experiments are performed using the Newer College and FusionPortable datasets to demonstrate the proposed NeLD-BA's state-of-the-art performance in multi-view point cloud registration and 3D mapping. We will open-source our code for the community.

</details>


### [145] [XS-VLA: Coupling Coarse-grained Spatial Distillation with Latent Flow Matching for Lightweight Robotic Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04171)
*Lei Iok Tong,Qingchen Xie,Wei Huang,Ying Jie Yap,Yujie Zhang,Qianzhi Li,Xiaolong Liu,Zhidong Deng*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: XS-VLA is a two-stage framework that uses knowledge distillation from a 4B LVLM into a 0.25B backbone plus latent flow matching policy to achieve efficient, spatially grounded robotic manipulation, outperforming sub-0.5B models on LIBERO with up to 7.2% success rate improvement and 3.2x speedup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LVLMs are computationally expensive for real-time robotic control, while lightweight models suffer from "spatial blindness" (weak native spatial prediction). Training VLA models on mixed human demonstrations degrades policy due to diverse behaviors.

Method: Two-stage framework: (1) Distill spatial semantic knowledge from Qwen3-VL-4B into SmolVLM2-0.25B via fine-tuning on coarse-grained spatial descriptions; (2) Condition a Latent Flow Matching policy (CVAE + Flow Matching) on the enhanced backbone to model multimodal action distributions.

Result: SOTA among sub-0.5B models on LIBERO; +7.2% average success rate, +23% on LIBERO-Long over SmolVLA 0.25B; outperforms 2.2B SmolVLA; 3.2x speedup over prior lightweight flow matching policy.

Conclusion: Spatial tuning and generative latent flow control substantially improve lightweight VLA performance, enabling efficient and spatially grounded robotic manipulation.

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) have shown strong multimodal understanding and spatial grounding, but their computational cost limits real-time robotic control. In contrast, lightweight models are suitable for edge deployment but often suffer from "spatial blindness", namely weak native spatial prediction ability. Training Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models on mixed human demonstrations can also degrade policy performance due to highly diverse behaviors. To address these limitations, we propose XS-VLA, a two-stage framework for efficient and spatially grounded robotic manipulation. First, we distill spatial semantic knowledge from Qwen3-VL-4B into the SmolVLM2-0.25B backbone by fine-tuning on curated coarse-grained spatial descriptions, turning the lightweight model into a spatially grounded engine. Second, we use this enhanced backbone to condition a Latent Flow Matching policy. Unlike deterministic controllers, our policy combines a Conditional Variational Autoencoder (CVAE) with Flow Matching dynamics to model complex multimodal action distributions. On the LIBERO benchmark, XS-VLA achieves state-of-the-art performance among models with fewer than 0.5B parameters. It improves average success rates by up to 7.2 percent, including a 23 percent gain on LIBERO-Long, over the SmolVLA 0.25B baseline, and outperforms the larger 2.2B vanilla SmolVLA. Ablations show that spatial tuning and generative latent flow control substantially improve lightweight VLA performance, delivering a 3.2 times speedup in mission execution over the previous lightweight flow matching policy.

</details>


### [146] [SoftVTBench: A Safety-Aware Visuo-Tactile Benchmark for Physically Constrained Robotic Manipulation of Deformable Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04234)
*Bowen Jing,Mingxin Wang,Ruiyang Hao,Chenchen Ge,Hanwen Shen,Junjie He,Yang Cui,Yiming Hou,Weitao Zhou,Jiawei Wang,Minglei Li,Dandan Zhang,Ding Zhao,Houde Liu,Xiaofan Li,Si Liu,Ping Luo,Haibao Yu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: SoftVTBench is a safety-aware visuo-tactile benchmark for deformable object manipulation in Isaac Sim, which separately evaluates Goal Success and Safety Success (no drop, deformation below threshold). Experiments show success-only metrics overstate performance, and tactile sensing improves safety (e.g., from 21.4% to 35.6% on deformable tasks).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing manipulation benchmarks are predominantly success-oriented and rarely evaluate whether a policy remains physically safe throughout execution (i.e., avoiding slip, drop, or excessive deformation).

Method: Built in Isaac Sim with FEM-simulated deformable objects, SoftVTBench provides multi-view RGB, tactile sensing, proprioception, and language instructions; defines four task suites over object type and variation axis; separately reports Goal Success and Safety Success (no drop + peak deformation below calibrated threshold from privileged FEM states). pi0.5-based baselines are implemented.

Result: Success-only evaluation overstates performance; many goal-completing rollouts violate safety. Tactile sensing improves Safety Success (21.4% → 35.6% on object-centric deformable tasks) and reduces deformation while maintaining comparable Goal Success.

Conclusion: SoftVTBench provides a reproducible benchmark for studying visuo-tactile deformable manipulation under physical interaction constraints, highlighting the importance of safety-aware evaluation.

Abstract: Deformable object manipulation poses challenges beyond task completion: successful execution must also maintain safe physical interaction, holding the object stably without slip or drop while avoiding excessive deformation. However, existing manipulation benchmarks are predominantly success-oriented and rarely evaluate whether a policy remains physically safe throughout execution. We present SoftVTBench, a safety-aware visuo-tactile benchmark for physically constrained deformable object manipulation. Built in Isaac Sim with finite-element-simulated deformable objects, SoftVTBench provides multi-view RGB observations, RGB tactile sensing with marker motion, proprioception, and language instructions, and defines four matched task suites over object type (deformable vs. rigid) and variation axis (object vs. spatial). It separately reports Goal Success and Safety Success; the latter additionally requires no drop and peak deformation below a calibrated object-specific threshold, measured from policy-hidden privileged Finite Element Method (FEM) states. We implement pi0.5-based baselines under this protocol. Experiments show that success-only evaluation substantially overstates policy performance, as a large fraction of goal-completing rollouts still violate physical safety. Furthermore, incorporating tactile sensing improves Safety Success (e.g., from 21.4% to 35.6% on object-centric deformable tasks) and reduces object deformation during execution, while maintaining comparable Goal Success. SoftVTBench provides a reproducible benchmark for studying visuo-tactile deformable manipulation under physical interaction constraints.

</details>


### [147] [Integrated Graph Search and Model Predictive Control for Smooth and Efficient Path Planning in Autonomous Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04259)
*Duc-Tien Bui,Ngoc Thinh Nguyen,Hung Duy Nguyen,Dong Bi,Tomislav Mihalj,Arno Eichberger*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A sequential path planning framework for autonomous vehicles that uses Dijkstra graph search to construct a lateral safety corridor, then refines the path via MPC optimization. It reduces computational cost by ~28-30% while improving smoothness (lower lateral acceleration, curvature, jerk) compared to polynomial fitting + QP baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Achieving safe, comfortable, and dynamically feasible paths with computational efficiency remains a significant challenge in autonomous vehicle path planning.

Method: (1) Dijkstra graph search on a discretized grid to obtain a rough path; (2) construct a spatially varying convex lateral safety corridor from the rough path; (3) formulate an MPC problem to refine the path within the corridor, penalizing the third-order spatial derivative of lateral offset over a prediction horizon.

Result: Consistently lower lateral acceleration, curvature, and jerk; computational cost reduced by 28.08% on straight roads and 29.52% on curved roads compared to polynomial fitting + QP baseline.

Conclusion: Exploiting graph-search structure within an MPC formulation provides an effective balance between path smoothness and computational efficiency for autonomous vehicles in structured driving environments.

Abstract: Path planning is a fundamental component of autonomous vehicles, where achieving safe, comfortable, and dynamically feasible paths while ensuring computational efficiency remains a significant challenge. This paper presents a sequential path planning framework in which a rough path obtained from graph search is explicitly exploited to guide a Model Predictive Control (MPC)-based path refinement. A rough path is first obtained via Dijkstra search on a discretized grid and is then used to construct a spatially varying convex lateral safety corridor that explicitly captures obstacle avoidance constraints, transforming discrete obstacle avoidance decisions into continuous feasibility constraints for optimization. Within this corridor, an MPC problem is formulated to refine the path, enabling efficient optimization while maintaining path smoothness by penalizing the third-order spatial derivative of the lateral offset over a prediction horizon. The proposed algorithm is evaluated in multiple overtaking scenarios on both straight and curved roads, including cases with single and multiple target vehicles, using high-fidelity environment simulations (i.e., CarMaker). Compared with the previous study, which used polynomial fitting and a quadratic programming method, the proposed approach consistently achieves lower lateral acceleration, curvature, and jerk while reducing computational cost by 28.08% on straight roads and 29.52% on curved roads. These results demonstrate that exploiting graph-search structure within an MPC formulation provides an effective balance between path smoothness and computational efficiency for autonomous vehicles in structured driving environments.

</details>


### [148] [FLOAT Drone for Physical Interaction: Lateral Airflow Reduction, Wrench Modeling, and Adaptive Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04260)
*Junxiao Lin,Kehan Zhou,Shuhang Ji,Yimin Peng,Shen Wang,Jialiang Hou,Fei Gao*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: FLOAT Drone is a fully actuated coaxial UAV with servo-driven control surfaces that enables 6-DoF wrench generation for close-proximity physical interaction, featuring reduced lateral airflow, high-fidelity aerodynamic modeling, and demonstrated drawer manipulation through a 2 cm handle clearance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aerial physical interaction requires UAVs that can exert contact forces while maintaining stable flight, demanding a platform with multidimensional wrench generation, compactness for confined spaces, and reduced lateral airflow toward targets during horizontal force application.

Method: A coaxial dual-rotor layout with servo-driven control surfaces immersed in rotor downwash generates 6-DoF forces and moments; a high-fidelity polynomial aerodynamic wrench model identified from precision force measurements captures nonlinear rotor-control-surface coupling; a constrained nonlinear allocator enables real-time wrench tracking.

Result: Force-matched CFD comparison shows reduced target-facing lateral airflow versus tilted-rotor alternatives; flight and interaction experiments demonstrate improved control accuracy over linear baselines, ground-effect and payload disturbance rejection, and successful drawer push-pull manipulation through a 2 cm handle clearance.

Conclusion: The FLOAT Drone framework validates that servo-driven control surfaces on a coaxial platform meet the coupled design requirements for close-proximity aerial physical interaction, achieving precise contact force exertion and manipulation in confined spaces.

Abstract: Aerial physical interaction represents a promising direction for next-generation unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs), but it requires an aerial platform that can exert contact forces while maintaining stable flight. For close-proximity tasks, this translates into three coupled design requirements: multidimensional wrench generation for stable contact, compactness for maneuverability and safety in confined spaces, and reduced lateral airflow toward the target when generating horizontal force. This article presents FLOAT Drone, a fully actuated coaxial UAV with servo-driven control surfaces for close-proximity physical interaction. The coaxial dual-rotor layout provides a compact propulsion layout, while the control surfaces, immersed in the rotor downwash, generate lateral forces and moments for 6-DoF wrench generation. A force-matched computational fluid dynamics (CFD) comparison with a tilted-rotor alternative quantifies the reduction in target-facing lateral airflow. To account for nonlinear rotor--control-surface coupling in the rotor wake, a high-fidelity polynomial aerodynamic wrench model is identified from precision force measurements and embedded in a constrained nonlinear allocator for real-time wrench tracking. Comparative flight and interaction experiments show that the proposed framework improves control accuracy over linear allocation baselines, rejects ground-effect and payload disturbances, and enables close-proximity drawer push--pull manipulation through a $2~\mathrm{cm}$ handle clearance.

</details>


### [149] [HALO-WA: Hybrid-Attention Latent-Guided Online Reinforcement Learning for World-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04265)
*Angen Ye,Weijie Ke,Xiaofeng Wang,Xinze Chen,Chaojun Ni,Guosheng Zhao,Boyuan Wang,Zheng Zhu,Junjie Xie,Dapeng Zhang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: HALO-WA is a hybrid-attention latent-guided online RL framework that adapts World-Action models to real-world precision manipulation tasks, improving success rates from 26.4% to 87.1% with only 45-75 minutes of online training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: World-Action (WA) models generate long-horizon action chunks for robotic manipulation but fail in real-world precision tasks due to calibration, perception, and contact-dynamics errors, especially in the final millimeters of alignment or insertion.

Method: HALO-WA introduces a hybrid-attention structure that preserves temporal consistency of action chunks while extracting task-relevant information from WA latents conditioned on visual context and end-stage correction needs. A lightweight actor-critic adapter enables fast online RL adaptation using latent features and action priors from the WA generation process.

Result: On four real-world precision manipulation tasks, HALO-WA improves average success rate from 26.4% (WA-base) to 87.1%, outperforming the strongest baseline by 19.2 percentage points, requiring only 45-75 minutes of online training per task. Supplementary simulation experiments conducted in RoboTwin.

Conclusion: HALO-WA effectively addresses the precision limitations of WA models through hybrid-attention latent-guided online RL, achieving significant improvements in real-world robotic manipulation tasks with minimal online training time.

Abstract: World-action (WA) models can generate long-horizon action chunks for general-purpose robotic manipulation, but they remain vulnerable to calibration, perception, and contact-dynamics errors in real-world precision tasks, often failing in the final few millimeters of alignment or insertion. We propose HALO-WA, a hybrid-attention latent-guided online reinforcement learning (RL) framework for WA models, which leverages latent features and action priors from the WA generation process through a lightweight actor-critic adapter to enable fast online adaptation to real deployment errors. HALO-WA introduces a hybrid-attention structure that preserves the temporal consistency of action chunks while reading task-relevant information from WA latents conditioned on visual context and end-stage correction requirements, thereby producing refined action chunks. We validate HALO-WA on four real-world precision manipulation tasks, where it improves the average success rate from 26.4\% for WA-base to 87.1\%, outperforming the strongest baseline by 19.2 percentage points while requiring only 45--75 minutes of online training per task. To facilitate reproducibility, we further conduct supplementary simulation experiments in RoboTwin and release the code at https://github.com/YeanRoot/HALO-WA.

</details>


### [150] [GPU-Accelerated Polygonal Signed Distance Functions for Real-Time Collision Avoidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04310)
*Taekwon Ga,Jongeun Choi*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes the Polygonal Signed Distance Function (PSDF), a geometry-exact, weight-free, branch-free signed distance function for convex polygonal robot footprints and polygonal obstacles. It enables batched GPU execution and automatic differentiation, and is embedded into an MPC framework (PSDF-MPC) via sequential quadratic programming. Real-time feasibility and robust collision avoidance in dense environments are demonstrated through simulations and real-world experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optimization-based local planning requires high-rate collision-avoidance constraint evaluation over a prediction horizon. In obstacle-dense environments, the computational workload of complex constraints becomes a bottleneck, motivating the need for collision-avoidance constraints that combine computational efficiency with geometric fidelity.

Method: The PSDF is a geometry-exact signed distance function between a convex polygonal robot footprint and obstacles represented by boundary edges, implemented as a weight-free, branch-free tensorized geometric pipeline for batched GPU execution and automatic differentiation. It is embedded into MPC by locally linearizing stage-wise safety constraints within a sequential quadratic programming-based real-time iteration scheme (PSDF-MPC). CPU/GPU computation is separated: GPU evaluates batched PSDF values and gradients, CPU solves a sparse QP.

Result: Microbenchmarks show PSDF scales favorably against signed-distance query baselines. Closed-loop simulated and real-world navigation experiments, including comparisons with optimization-based baselines, demonstrate that PSDF-MPC maintains real-time feasibility and robust collision avoidance in dense polygonal environments.

Conclusion: PSDF-MPC achieves real-time feasibility and robust collision avoidance in dense polygonal environments by leveraging a geometry-exact, GPU-friendly signed distance function within an MPC framework, effectively separating GPU evaluation from CPU optimization.

Abstract: Optimization-based local planning and control require high-rate collision-avoidance constraint evaluation over a prediction horizon. In obstacle-dense environments, where feasible space is limited and the constraints become increasingly complex, the computational workload often dominates the control-cycle runtime. The resulting bottleneck motivates collision-avoidance constraints that combine computational efficiency with geometric fidelity. The proposed Polygonal Signed Distance Function (PSDF) is a geometry-exact signed distance function between a convex polygonal robot footprint and obstacles represented by their boundary edges. It is implemented as a weight-free, branch-free tensorized geometric pipeline enabling batched GPU execution and automatic differentiation. The PSDF is embedded into model predictive control by locally linearizing the stage-wise safety constraints within a sequential quadratic programming-based real-time iteration scheme, yielding the PSDF-embedded model predictive controller (PSDF-MPC). The design separates CPU/GPU computation so that the GPU evaluates batched PSDF values and gradients while the CPU solves a sparse quadratic program whose dimension is determined by system dimensions and horizon length, not by obstacle features. Microbenchmarks show that PSDF scales favorably against signed-distance query baselines. Closed-loop simulated and real-world navigation experiments, including comparisons with optimization-based baselines, demonstrate that PSDF-MPC maintains real-time feasibility and robust collision avoidance in dense polygonal environments.

</details>


### [151] [Agent-driven Long-tail Simulation for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04331)
*Junru Gu,Lijin Yang,Jianing Huang,Shu Liu,Zhongzhan Huang,Hang Zhao*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper proposes an agent-driven simulation framework using instruction-following LLMs to control surrounding traffic agents, and introduces SemanticPlan, a closed-loop planning benchmark with long-tail, semantically rich scenarios. Experiments show SOTA planners struggle in these challenging settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing closed-loop simulators for autonomous driving rely on log replay or rule-based agents, which lack behavioral diversity and long-tail scenario coverage.

Method: A simulation framework where surrounding agents are controlled by instruction-following LLMs via a structured action interface, enabling intentional and reactive behaviors with physical plausibility. SemanticPlan benchmark augments nuPlan scenes with multiple interactive agents following diverse language instructions.

Result: State-of-the-art planners fail to consistently achieve safe and effective task completion in these long-tail scenarios.

Conclusion: Long-tail, semantically rich scenarios remain challenging for current autonomous driving planners, highlighting the need for more robust closed-loop planning methods.

Abstract: Evaluating autonomous driving systems in closed-loop settings requires realistic and interactive simulation, yet existing simulators largely rely on log replay or rule-based agents, limiting behavioral diversity and long-tail coverage. We propose an agent-driven simulation framework in which surrounding road participants are controlled by instruction-following large language models through a structured action interface, enabling intentional and reactive behaviors while preserving physical plausibility. Furthermore, we introduce SemanticPlan, a benchmark of closed-loop planning in long-tail and semantically rich scenarios that augment real nuPlan scenes with multiple interactive agents following diverse language instructions. Evaluation results show that state-of-the-art planners still struggle to consistently achieve safe and effective task completion, suggesting that these long-tail scenarios remain challenging.

</details>


### [152] [A Perception-Manipulation Robotics System for Food Cutting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04367)
*Xinyuan Luo,Wenzhen Yuan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A perception-manipulation framework for food-cutting robots that uses force data from a trial cut to select the optimal knife, then applies reinforcement learning to adapt cutting speed and energy efficiency, achieving 100% knife selection accuracy on unseen food and human-comparable performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cooking robots need to cut diverse foods with varying properties, but different foods require distinct cutting policies and knife choices. This creates a challenge for developing versatile robotic cutting systems.

Method: A two-stage framework: (1) a knife selection module that uses force data from a preliminary fixed trial cut to select the optimal knife; (2) an adaptive cutting phase using reinforcement learning to balance cutting speed and energy efficiency.

Result: Knife selection module achieved 100% success rate on unseen food. The combined method achieved high performance comparable to human operators.

Conclusion: The proposed perception-manipulation framework effectively handles diverse food-cutting tasks by combining intelligent knife selection with adaptive RL-based cutting, achieving human-competitive performance.

Abstract: In the development of cooking robots, mastering the task of cutting is crucial. A significant challenge lies in the diverse properties of food, which necessitate distinct cutting policies and even different knives for optimal processing. This paper presents a perception-manipulation framework for food-cutting tasks. Our system features a knife selection module that utilizes force data from a preliminary fixed trial cut to select the appropriate knife for the given food. This is followed by an adaptive cutting phase using reinforcement learning (RL) to balance cutting speed and energy efficiency. In our experiments, the knife selection module achieved 100% successful rate on unseen food, and we compared the performances of fixed policy, RL policy, with human operators. Our method not only achieves high performance but also demonstrates comparable results to those of human participants.

</details>


### [153] [SurgAM: Surgical Affordance Map Prediction with Multimodal Feature Fusion for Robot Autonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04378)
*Lei Song,Yonghao Long,Mengya Xu,Jiayi Geng,Xiuyuan Chen,Qi Dou*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper introduces surgical affordance prediction for identifying actionable regions for fundamental surgical actions. The authors propose an adaptive feature fusion framework combining a self-supervised ViT encoder and a generative model encoder, with hierarchical prompt learning and a scene-guided attention decoder. A new dataset with affordance annotations for aspiration, clipping, and retraction is established. The method achieves SOTA performance and enables autonomous surgical actions on realistic phantoms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridging visual scene understanding with autonomous action planning in surgical automation remains a fundamental challenge. While scene perception (tool recognition, segmentation) is well-studied, understanding and predicting actionable possibilities for surgical automation is underexplored.

Method: (1) An adaptive feature fusion framework combining a self-supervised vision transformer encoder (semantic understanding) with a large-scale generative model encoder (spatial awareness). (2) Hierarchical prompt learning for adapting to varying procedural contexts. (3) A scene-guided attention decoder to focus on critical areas while suppressing background distractions.

Result: State-of-the-art performance on a new affordance-annotated dataset derived from public surgical datasets for three actions (aspiration, clipping, retraction). The predicted affordance maps successfully enable autonomous surgical actions on realistic lung and prostate phantoms in downstream automation tasks.

Conclusion: Surgical affordance prediction is a viable approach for bridging perception and action in surgical automation. The proposed framework effectively integrates complementary visual features and demonstrates practical applicability for downstream autonomous surgical tasks.

Abstract: Surgical automation is being increasingly studied, yet bridging visual scene understanding with autonomous action planning remains a fundamental challenge. While much research effort has been made on scene perception (e.g., tool recognition and scene segmentation), understanding and predicting actionable possibilities for surgical automation is still underexplored. In this paper, we introduce surgical affordance prediction, which identifies actionable regions for fundamental surgical actions from visual data.
